### L1.19 Spec: collinear vertices in cut contours (opening classification, 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Evidence (Hospital 85-deg wing, after L1.18, Node, `GAP:opening_unclassified` = 257):** 256 of 257 are ONE shape: a closed `A-DOOR` LWPOLYLINE of **9 vertices** (e.g. 915 x 51 mm: corners plus extra vertices lying on the straight edges, such as `[42706.3, 83499.4]` between `[42706.3, 83521.7]` and `[42706.3, 83470.7]`), 1 of 15 vertices. They are the plan section of a door leaf cut by `G.elementContours`: the mesh splits one straight edge into several collinear pieces (leaf, frame, casing meet on one line). The reader's rectangle test (`dxf2bim.js` W4: 4 corners, 2 parallel + 4 perpendicular pairs) counts segments, so a rectangle with 5 extra vertices is "unclassified": 9 unconsumed parts, one gap per door. The Duplex doors are 4-vertex rectangles and classify.
**Rule (L1.19; CORRECTED 2026-10-17: "lossless" withdrawn, see L1.26 results):** in a wing set a cut contour drops every vertex that lies on the straight line through its two neighbours (perpendicular distance <= 0.001 mm, the DXF's own resolution); the polygon stays the same to within that tolerance per removed vertex (extents identical, area change at most 0.105 mm2 measured). Logged `§COLLINEAR_DROP guid=<g> cls=<c> before=<n> after=<m>`. Wing sets only for now (`model.perStoreyEnv` family); promoted to the Python oracle and the straight path once it holds with NO change on Duplex, SampleCastle and HHS (W-LOG/W-ENT unchanged).
**Witnesses (issue each proves):**
- W-L119-OPEN: Hospital 85-deg wing `GAP:opening_unclassified` falls from 257; the count that remains is listed with its cause (proves the shape was the cause, not a symptom).
- W-L119-AREA: every changed contour has the same area (1e-6 mm2 relative) and the same bbox as before (proves lossless).
- W-L119-CONTROL: a contour with a REAL 5 deg kink is NOT changed (proves it removes collinear points only, never straightens).
- Straight-set parity (W-LOG/W-ENT) and the Duplex wing witnesses unchanged.
**Not claimed:** `GAP:no_host_cut` (142), `no_elevation_height` (175), `wall_below_min_thickness` (58) are separate causes; the first may follow once doors classify (a classified door cuts its host), the others get their own measurement after this rule.

#### L1.19 Results (2026-10-17)
Built as specified (wing sets only; `dropCollinear`, `§COLLINEAR_DROP` log lines). **W-L119-AREA** pass (8 vertices -> 4, area and extent equal), **W-L119-CONTROL** pass (a 4 mm bend kept), straight-set parity W-LOG/W-ENT unchanged (py=318 = js), Duplex wing witnesses unchanged. sw.js v95.

The tiny ones are door parts, not openings. Next rule candidate (spec first): union the touching contours of ONE element into its outline (polygon-clipping is already vendored), logged per door, so the reader sees one outline per door as it does on the Duplex; witness = the union has the same area as the sum of the non-overlapping parts.

### L1.20 Spec: one outline per door/window (union of touching contours of ONE element), 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code
**Evidence (Hospital 85-deg wing after L1.19):** 160 `GAP:opening_unclassified` remain, all closed 4-vertex `A-DOOR` rectangles of 76 x 25 / 25 x 76 / 915 x 51 / 813 x 51 mm; the first plan holds 413 `A-DOOR` contours for its doors: one door element is cut into many parts (leaf, frame sides, casing returns), each part its own contour, so the reader sees many small rectangles that are not an opening.
**Rule (wing sets only, same flag as L1.18/L1.19; promoted to the Python oracle only when Duplex/SampleCastle/HHS show NO change):** for an IfcDoor / IfcWindow element with 2 or more contours on one plan, the contours are united (`polygon-clipping` union, vendored 0.15.7) into the outline(s) they form. A union result with a HOLE is not used (the element keeps its parts, logged `§OPEN_UNION_HOLE`); a result with more than one disjoint ring keeps those rings (logged). Logged per element `§OPEN_UNION guid=<g> cls=<c> parts=<n> -> rings=<m> area_sum=<a> area_union=<b>`. Walls and columns are NOT united (their parts are real separate hatched pieces, and the sliver / collinear rules already cover them).
**Witnesses:**
- W-L120-AREA: for every united element, union area <= sum of part areas and the union bbox == the bbox of the parts (proves nothing is lost or grown); where the parts do not overlap, union area == sum (to 1e-6 relative).
- W-L120-CONTROL: two DISJOINT contours of one element stay two rings (the union does not bridge a gap).
- W-L120-OPEN: Hospital 85-deg wing `GAP:opening_unclassified` falls from 160; the remainder is listed by shape and cause.
- Unchanged: straight-set parity (W-LOG/W-ENT), Duplex wing witnesses, W-L119.
**Not claimed:** `no_dimension` (97, needs a stated sill/height), `no_host_cut` 142, `no_elevation_height` 175, `wall_below_min_thickness` 58.

**L1.20b (amendment, measured the same day, BEFORE its code):** the union alone does not help: of 189 united Hospital door/window elements, 176 stay MULTI-ring because their parts are disjoint (no union bug: `union area == sum of parts`, max difference 0.30 mm2). Measured example: a door element of bbox 965 x 173 mm = a 813 x 51 leaf plus four 76 x 25 casing corner blocks at the four corners of the bbox; another is 1982 x 173 = two 915 x 51 leaves plus four corner blocks. The corner blocks are casing returns, not openings.
- **Rule:** in a wing set, when the parts of one door/window element remain more than one ring after the union, the element is drawn as ONE rectangle: its own plan bbox (the element's IFC world bbox, in the wing frame). This is the same shape the writer already draws for an opening above or below the cut (`planOpenings`), so the convention exists. Condition: every part is an axis-aligned rectangle (4 vertices, edges on X/Y within 0.001 mm); otherwise the parts are kept (`§OPEN_BBOX_SKIP reason=part_not_axis_rect`). The bbox contains every part by construction (checked, logged). Logged `§OPEN_BBOX guid=<g> cls=<c> parts=<n> bbox=<w> x <d> mm`.
- **What it adds (stated):** the drawn outline covers the gaps between the casing blocks and the leaf: area not in the section. It is the footprint of the door product with its casing, not the cut.
- **Witnesses:** W-L120B-CONTAIN (every replaced element's bbox contains all its parts and equals the extent of the parts); W-L120B-CONTROL (an element with a rotated part is NOT replaced); W-L120B-OPEN (Hospital 85-deg wing `GAP:opening_unclassified` falls from 129; remainder listed); Duplex wing and straight-set parity unchanged.

#### L1.20 / L1.20b Result: REVERTED (2026-10-17), and why
Built as specified and measured: Hospital 85-deg wing `opening_unclassified` 257 -> 0 (union: 189 elements, 0 area violations; bbox for the 176 disjoint ones; Duplex wing witnesses at 4 angles and W-L116-BACK B2 still equal). **The browser witness W-E2E-DXF-WING then failed** (5 PASS / 1 FAIL): the Duplex wing set, which qualified before, was `blocked` with `GAP:no_dimension` 12 + `GAP:fill_size_mismatch` 12. Cause: merging a door's parts into one outline removes the shape the reader uses to recognise a DOOR (contour + leaf line + arc), so each door is read as a plain rectangular opening that needs a stated sill/height. The Node witnesses did not see it (they compare bodies, with no library, and never read the verdict); only the browser path with the library did. So the Hospital's "0 unclassified" was hollow: those doors became generic rectangles (`no_dimension` 94 -> 174), not doors.
- **Reverted** in BIMCompiler (`feat/wing-frame`, revert commit 3c10009f7); L1.19 (collinear vertices, lossless) stays. The Duplex wing e2e is back to 7/7.
- **Lesson recorded:** a rule that changes what a door looks like must be witnessed through the reader's VERDICT (qualify), not only through bodies. Add the verdict comparison (wing vs straight) to `witness_dxf_wing_back.js` before any further door/window outline rule.
- **Open, honestly:** the 160 Hospital door parts (leaf 813 x 51 and four 76 x 25 casing corner blocks per door) are not recognised as doors by the reader, and a union/bbox is the wrong fix. The reader's door model (hinge arc + leaf) needs to be read first to see what it expects from a multi-part door (`door_part_ambiguous` 16 is its own signal). Next step is reading `dxf2bim.js` door classification, not drawing.
- **Also fixed here (found by the browser run):** the WING block of the export panel was nested inside the SHEETS block, so the sheets' select handlers also fired on the wing select and broke the redraw ("grid 0 x 0"); it is its own block now. sw.js v97.

### L1.21 Spec: a door WITHOUT a stated swing is drawn as its footprint, not as its section parts (2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Evidence (read from `dxf2bim.js` W-phase and measured on the Hospital 85-deg wing, Level 1):** the reader recognises a door from its swing ARC (hinge = centre, width = radius) plus parts: entities touching the hinge/arc end, and entities inside the door's opening box (the gap between drawn wall pieces). The 55 Level-1 doors that HAVE an arc all get an `opening_box` and 21 parts each (55/55): the rule works. The unclassified openings belong to doors with NO arc: the writer logs `GAP:door_hinge` (43) / `door_swing` (2) because the IFC2x3 model states no operation for them, so the plan draws only their section parts (a leaf rectangle + four 76 x 25 mm casing corner blocks). With no arc the reader reads the leaf as a rectangular opening (`GAP:no_dimension`: sill/height not stated) and each corner block as an unclassified rectangle. L1.20 (merge parts of ALL doors) broke the Duplex because it also changed doors that DO have an arc; that is why it was reverted.
**Rule (wing sets only, same flag):** for an IfcDoor whose swing cannot be drawn (the same `doorHinge` / `doorHingeIfc` result that makes the writer log `door_hinge` / `door_swing`), its plan contours are replaced by ONE rectangle: the extent of its own parts (= its footprint with casing), provided every part is an axis-aligned rectangle (else the parts are kept, `§DOOR_NOSWING_SKIP`). A door WITH an arc is not touched (so the Duplex and every door the reader already handles are unchanged). Logged `§DOOR_NOSWING_BBOX guid=<g> parts=<n> bbox=<w> x <d> mm`. It states nothing new: the door still has no swing and no sill/height; it becomes ONE honest opening that asks for its dimension (`GAP:no_dimension`, a number the user or the door schedule supplies), not 5 unclassified pieces.
**Witnesses:**
- W-L121-DUPLEX (the lesson of L1.20): the Duplex wing set is byte-identical before and after (every Duplex door has an arc), AND the wing verdict equals the straight verdict (same blocking codes) in `witness_dxf_wing_back.js` B4, AND the browser W-E2E-DXF-WING stays 7/7.
- W-L121-OPEN: Hospital 85-deg wing `GAP:opening_unclassified` falls; the remainder listed by shape and cause; `no_dimension` rises by the number of swingless doors (stated, expected).
- W-L121-CONTROL: a door WITH an arc keeps its parts; a swingless door with a tilted part keeps its parts.
**Not claimed:** the Hospital qualifying (sill/height, `no_host_cut` 142, `no_elevation_height` 175, `wall_below_min_thickness` 58 remain).

### L1.22 Spec: round columns in a wing (found by the HHS check, 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Finding:** run on the merged HHS model (112 walls, 257 columns), the straight set is read (blocked, HHS's known gaps) but the wing set at its own angle (0 deg) is REFUSED: `missing GRIDX(>=2)`. Cause: `frameModel` keeps a column only when its principal angle is consistent (q >= 0.5) and matches; all 257 HHS columns are ROUND (90 vertices, face-normal quality q = 2e-7: no direction at all), so all 257 were dropped as "unframed" and with them the grid evidence (the grid of a building without footing walls comes from its columns). The Duplex has no columns, and the Hospital run never showed it as a refusal (its walls carried the grid), so the Duplex-only falsifier could not see this.
**Rule (wing sets only):** a column that is rotationally symmetric (the existing `columnAxis` test: all plan vertices at equal radius, or point-symmetric: the same test the writer already uses to give a column its centre) has no direction, so the angle rule cannot decide for it. It is KEPT in the wing when its centre lies inside the extent (plan AABB) of the wing's kept walls and slabs; it is rotated with the wing like everything else. A column WITH a direction follows the angle rule as before. Logged `§WING_COLUMNS_SYMMETRIC kept=<n> outside_extent=<m>`.
- **Stated limit:** when two wings' extents overlap, a symmetric column inside both is drawn in both wing sets. No double counting arises today because wing sets are exported and imported one at a time; the rule for assembling several wings in one model (one owner per column, nearest wing axis) is part of the multi-wing assembly, not built.
**Witnesses:**
- W-L122-GRID: HHS (merged) wing set at 0 deg is no longer refused for the grid; its grid equals the straight set's grid (labels and positions).
- W-L122-VS-STRAIGHT: `witness_dxf_wing_vs_straight.js`: for a rectilinear model the reader's blocking codes with counts on the wing set equal the straight set's, OR every difference is listed against the rule that causes it (no silent difference). Run on Duplex and HHS (merged).
- W-L122-CONTROL: a model with no symmetric columns (Duplex) unchanged.

**L1.22b (amendment, measured the same day, BEFORE its code):** `witness_dxf_wing_vs_straight.js` V2 FAILED on HHS: the straight grid has 14 X lines, the wing 12 (`-12.704` and `53.033` missing): round columns standing OUTSIDE the plan extent of the walls (canopy / edge columns) were dropped by the "centre inside the extent" rule, and the building grid is built from them. The extent rule is the wrong test.
- **Rule (replaces the extent test):** a symmetric column belongs to the wing of its NEAREST wall: the wall (of any angle, kept or not) with the smallest plan-bbox distance to the column centre (0 when inside; ties to the smaller wall guid). It is kept when that wall is in the wing's kept set. This gives ONE owner per symmetric column even when wings overlap (closes the "stated limit" above) and keeps edge columns with the building they stand next to. Logged as before plus `§WING_COLUMN_OWNER guid=<g> nearest=<wall> dist_mm=<d>` only for a column whose nearest wall is NOT in this wing (so the log is small).
- **Witness unchanged:** V1 read, V2 same grid, V3 not worse, V4 differences listed, on Duplex and HHS (merged).

### L1.23 Spec: curtain walls in a wing (HHS V2, 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Finding (HHS merged, wing at 0 deg vs straight):** the straight grid has 14 X lines, the wing 12; the two missing lines (`x = -12.704`, `x = 53.033`) are `§CURTAIN_GRID` lines: the straight set grids a curtain wall that is an exterior run (`gridded=True`). `frameModel` sets `model.curtains = []`, so a wing set drops EVERY curtain wall, their grid, their plan bands, their elevation rectangles. The round-column rule (L1.22b) was not the cause (257 of 257 columns kept, 0 owned by another wing).
**Rule (wing sets only):** a curtain wall is framed like a wall: the angle of a curtain wall is the most common (1-deg bin) consistent angle of its plate/member parts (`elementAngle`, q >= 0.5); it is kept in the wing when that angle is within tol of the wing angle (mod 90); a curtain wall with no consistent part direction is NOT framed (counted). A kept curtain wall keeps all its parts and its door parts (doors follow their host, the curtain wall). Logged `§WING_CURTAINS kept=<n> unframed=<m>`.
**Witnesses:** W-L123-GRID: HHS wing grid == straight grid (V2 of `witness_dxf_wing_vs_straight.js`, currently FAIL, must PASS); V1/V3/V4 unchanged; Duplex (no curtain walls) unchanged; Hospital wing curtain counts reported.

### L1.24 Spec: a wall whose base is below the storey level states its base (2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured (Hospital 85-deg wing):** `GAP:no_elevation_height` = 228 in the verdict (A-WALL-FULL 47 on Level 6 alone, A-WALL-PRTN, A-WALL-CURT, a few A-DOOR). The causes are several; ONE is specifiable and countable: **49 of 594 wing walls start below their storey level** (base - level = -0.15, -0.2, -0.5, -1.0 m; 23 of them are refused by the marking writer: `WALLTOP wall <g>: base 165.6112 is not the storey level, no marking (gap stays)`). The reader assumes base = storey level for a stated top (`applyWallTops` sets `base: 0`), so the writer rightly writes nothing and the gap stays. Other causes (pieces of one wall, exterior pieces whose elevation rectangle starts at the wall's real base, curtain bands) are NOT claimed by this rule and are measured after it.
**Rule:** `@WALLTOP=handle:<h>,<z_top_m>[,<z_base_m>]`: the optional third value is the wall's base elevation in metres (absolute, like the top). Reader: `base = z_base - level` (so -0.15 m below the level), `height = z_top - z_base`; without it, unchanged (`base 0`). A per-handle marking with a base wins over the storey rule `slab_above` for that wall (the storey rule would otherwise give it `base 0`). Writer: a wall whose base differs from the storey level by more than `ELEV_TOL` gets the 3-value marking, with the base FROM the model (IFC wall z0); a wall with base == level keeps the 2-value marking (so Duplex output is byte-identical). `MARK:conflict` for a handle with two different tops or bases; malformed when the third value is not a finite number.
**Witnesses:** W-L124-PARSE (3-value parses, 2-value unchanged, non-numeric rejected, conflict on a differing base); W-L124-APPLY (`applyWallTops` with a base: base = z_base - level, height = z_top - z_base; without: base 0; per-handle base beats the storey rule); W-L124-DUPLEX (Duplex export byte-identical: no base ever differs); W-L124-OPEN (Hospital 85-deg wing `no_elevation_height` falls by the walls with a stated base; remainder listed by layer and storey).

Straight-set parity W-LOG/W-ENT, wing witnesses and HHS V1-V4 unchanged (HHS wing 600 vs straight 631). sw.js v100.
- **W-L124-OPEN:** Hospital 85-deg wing `GAP:no_elevation_height` **228 -> 188**, total blocking 685 -> 645. The other 188 are not base offsets: exterior pieces whose elevation rectangle does not start at the storey level, wall pieces split by openings, curtain bands, doors; each needs its own measurement.
- Not browser-run (the Duplex marks are byte-identical, so the browser path sees the same bytes).

### L1.25 Spec: two door types at one plan point are not tagged (export defect found by the HHS/SampleCastle check, 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Measured (SampleCastle, straight export):** the reader REFUSES 2 of 10 sheets (`MARK:conflict @TAG door at (2609.99,21325) has two texts: 01 plaatmateriaal - multiplex / 01 Hout - stelkozijn`, 4 such points), so the whole set cannot be read. 205 doors; 12 plan points hold more than one door element (a door modelled as several `IfcDoor` products, e.g. panel + frame, at the same bbox centre); at 4 of them the products have DIFFERENT types, and `planDoors` writes one `@TAG=door,<type>` per element at that point: two different texts at one point = a marking conflict by the reader's own rule. The same-type points (8) write identical texts and are fine.
**Rule (writer, all sets):** at a plan point (door bbox centre, 0.001 mm) where the door elements carry more than one distinct type, NO `@TAG` is written for that point and the writer logs `§MARK_GAP TAG door at (x,y): types <a> / <b> differ, not tagged`; the doors stay untagged and the reader reports them as its own gap (stated, not guessed). Same-type points are unchanged (the identical text is still written for each element). Nothing else in the marking changes.
**Witnesses:** W-L125-READ: SampleCastle straight export has NO sheet refused for `MARK:conflict` (all 10 sheets get a role); W-L125-NOCHANGE: Duplex marks byte-identical (no point there has two types) and HHS marks byte-identical; W-L125-CONTROL: a synthetic point with two types writes no tag and logs the gap, a point with two doors of the SAME type still writes its tag.
**Not claimed:** SampleCastle qualifying (its other gaps stay); which of the two types is "the door" (a product-family decision, not the writer's).

### L1.26 Spec: promote the wing-only writer rules to every set, and to the Python oracle (decided 2026-10-17; spec BEFORE code)
**Why:** the wing-only rules were built for non-rectilinear buildings but helped a real rectilinear one (HHS: wing 600 vs straight 631 blocking, every difference listed), and SampleCastle's straight export now stops at the very rule L1.18 solved for wings (`@LEVEL=0 is not a drawn elevation boundary`). Two code paths for one building is also a drift risk.
**Rules promoted (definitions exactly as in the sections named; the framing itself, L1.16/L1.22/L1.23 and `@FRAME`, stays wing-only because it needs an angle):**
- **L1.17** R3 judged on long faces (area-weighted >= 90 % of vertical-face area on the wall's axes); the contour is unchanged; log `§ENDFACE_R3` per such wall.
- **L1.18** elevations draw each storey's own envelope walls (the walls on THAT storey's envelope face), not only the grid storey's.
- **L1.19** collinear vertices (<= 0.001 mm) dropped from every cut contour (lossless); log `§COLLINEAR_DROP`.
- **L1.21** a door whose swing cannot be drawn is ONE footprint rectangle when all its parts are axis-aligned rectangles (else `§DOOR_NOSWING_SKIP`); a door with an arc is never touched; log `§DOOR_NOSWING_BBOX`.
(L1.24 `@WALLTOP` base and L1.25 door-tag points are already general.)
**Order of work (each step a witness before the next):**
1. Python `std2d.py` gets the four rules (same constants, same log lines, same order of operations as the JS).
2. JS flags default ON for every set (`model.longFaceR3`, `model.perStoreyEnv` set in `loadModel`; collinear and swingless-door rules follow the same flag); wing sets unchanged.
3. Parity: `witness_std2d_set.js` W-LOG + W-ENT (Python vs JS) on Duplex, SampleCastle, HHS (merged) and the Hospital, each in the SAME run as the old numbers: the log families `§ENDFACE_R3`, `§COLLINEAR_DROP`, `§DOOR_NOSWING_*` equal line for line.
4. Regression ladder, all must hold: Duplex straight set byte-identical to before (no rule fires there: the evidence is the wing witnesses), `witness_dxf_export_roundtrip.js` QF blocking 0, `witness_dxf_wing_vs_straight.js` V1-V4 on Duplex + HHS (the straight set now takes the same rules, so the differences list shrinks to the framing only), W-E2E-DXF-EXPORT 14/14 and W-E2E-DXF-WING 7/7 in the browser.
5. W-L126-SC: SampleCastle straight set is read AND reaches a verdict (the LV refusal is gone); the verdict and its blocking codes are listed, not claimed good.
**Falsifier:** each rule is switchable off (`STD2D_NO_PROMOTE=<rule>` env for the tests only); with the rule off the SampleCastle LV refusal must return (proves L1.18 is what removed it).
**Not claimed:** any model qualifying (Hospital 645, SampleCastle and HHS keep their own gaps).

#### L1.26 Results (2026-10-17; BIMCompiler #206, master 22e4b59ae)
Built as specified: Python oracle + JS carry the same four rules, same constants and log lines; flags default ON for every set; `STD2D_NO_PROMOTE=<l117,l118,l119,l121>` switches them off (tests only). Wing framing stays wing-only. sw.js v102, `modeller/dxf2bim` re-synced to master.

- **Honest correction of the spec's plan:** a Duplex straight set is NOT byte-identical (237 polylines lose 1218 collinear vertices; extents identical, relative area change 1.7e-13). Collinear drop on SampleCastle/HHS changes area by at most 0.105 mm2 (4.4e-5 relative), extents identical: "lossless" is withdrawn from L1.19, the claim is "within 0.001 mm per removed vertex". The marking bytes of the Duplex are unchanged.
- **Ladder (step 4):** `witness_dxf_export_roundtrip.js` QF 0 / pipeline gaps 0, `witness_dxf_scopes_merge.js`, `witness_dxf_wing_back.js` B2 + B4 (30, 85 deg), `witness_dxf_wing_vs_straight.js` V1-V4 on Duplex (36 = 36) and HHS (straight 600 == wing 600, differences: none; before promotion straight 631), browser W-E2E-DXF-EXPORT 14/14 and W-E2E-DXF-WING 7/7.

- **Step 5 (W-L126-SC) FAILED and the spec's reason was WRONG:** SampleCastle still stops at `@LEVEL=0 is not a drawn elevation boundary`; L1.18 does not remove it. Measured: SampleCastle's walls start 0.09 m BELOW their storey level (ground walls -0.09..2.62, upper storeys 2.91 for level 3.0; 48 ground pieces at -0.24, foundation -0.75), so no elevation wall rectangle has z0 = level within tolerance, in any storey. It is the base-below-level problem (L1.24) in the ELEVATION path, not the stepped outline. The straight SampleCastle set is read (all 10 sheets get a role since L1.25, gate ok) but Convert refuses.
- **Next, spec first:** the reader's LV rule (`dxf2bim_import.js`, BASIC_SHAPES_SPEC §2.3) accepts a level as drawn only when an elevation wall rectangle starts at it. The writer DOES draw every storey's datum line in the elevations (`levelsDrawn`, from the model's storeys). Candidate rule: a level is drawn when an elevation wall rectangle starts at it OR a drawn level datum line of that elevation exists; wall heights then come from the per-handle `@WALLTOP` base (L1.24), not from the elevation rectangle. This is a READER semantic change (BIMCompiler `docs/DXF2BIM_SPEC.md`) and needs the user's nod and the Duplex verdict witnesses (B4-style) before it ships.
- Noted: the wing e2e logs one benign 404 for the optional git-ignored `dxf2bim/local/library_subset_duplex.json` in a fresh worktree; the library falls back to OCI.

### L1.27 Spec: a plan level is "drawn" by a wall boundary OR a drawn level datum (reader change, approved by the user 2026-10-17 "OK"; spec BEFORE code)
**Evidence:** SampleCastle (straight set, all 10 sheets have a role since L1.25) is refused by Convert: `@LEVEL=0 is not a drawn elevation boundary (BASIC_SHAPES_SPEC §2.3 LV)`. Its walls start 0.09 m BELOW their storey level in every storey, so no elevation wall rectangle has `z0 = level`; the Hospital has the same (-0.15 / -0.2 / -0.5 / -1.0). The writer draws every storey's datum line in each elevation (`levelsDrawn`, A-ANNO-LEVL, from the model's own storeys) and the reader already reads those lines (`elevs[].lines` from `levelLinesZ`), but the LV rule ignores them.
**Rule (reader, `dxf2bim_import.js`; BIMCompiler `docs/DXF2BIM_BASIC_SHAPES_SPEC.md` §2.3 LV amended in the same change):**
- A plan `@LEVEL` is accepted when (a) an elevation wall rectangle has `z0` equal to it within `elev_tol` (unchanged), OR (b) a drawn level datum line of an elevation lies at it within `elev_tol`. (b) is evidence that the level exists in the drawing; it states NOTHING about wall bases.
- Wall heights are unchanged: `applyWallHeights` still matches only rectangles that start at the level; a wall with another base takes its height from its per-handle `@WALLTOP` (with the stated base, L1.24) or stays `GAP:no_elevation_height`. Nothing is guessed.
- The top of the TOP storey: unchanged when a rectangle starts at its level; otherwise the lowest drawn datum line above it (beyond `elev_tol`) in any elevation; none -> refused, reason `no drawn level above the top storey`.
- The evidence used is recorded per storey (`levelDrawn` = rectangle handles, new `levelDatum` = datum line handles) and shown in the report.
**Witnesses:**
- W-L127-DUPLEX: every existing importer witness is unchanged (the Duplex has BOTH a rectangle and a datum at each level, so rule (a) decides exactly as before): `witness_dxf_import_node.js` P1-P8, `witness_dxf_wing_back.js` B2+B4, `witness_dxf_export_roundtrip.js`, browser W-E2E-DXF-EXPORT 14/14 and W-E2E-DXF-WING 7/7.
- W-L127-SC: SampleCastle straight set: Convert reaches a verdict (not refused); the verdict and blocking codes are LISTED, not claimed good.
- W-L127-CONTROL: a level with neither a rectangle nor a datum (SampleCastle with `@LEVEL` moved to 4.2) is still refused with the LV reason; the top storey with no datum above it is refused with the new reason.
**Not claimed:** SampleCastle qualifying; the heights of walls whose base differs from the level (they stay gaps or take `@WALLTOP`).

## Order
basic shapes (running) → L1 → L2 → L3 → L4 → L5 (after L1.6 acid test) → film (FILM_PLAN §10), English first.

## L7 Generic CAD import: pre-processor, defaults sheet, provenance, IFC typing (spec 2026-10-18, written BEFORE code; user suggestions of the same day)
**Why.** The real-CAD shed blind test (neutral name: "shed") worked only through a one-off script (`shed_scene.js`) that hard-codes that drawing. Two defects showed what every CAD set will meet: (1) ONE DXF entity with several loops (a HATCH) gave several bodies the SAME guid, so meshes were swapped (inward/odd triangles); (2) values the drawing does not state (roof sheet thickness, truss spacing) were silently defaulted.
**L7.1 Pre-processor `dxf2prep.js` (generic, runs before role/gate; browser + Node; pure functions).**
- (a) **Unique ids:** every body gets `<handle>[-L<loop>]`; the module ASSERTS uniqueness over a whole set and throws on a collision (falsifier: feeding two bodies the same id must fail).
- (b) **CAD line loops:** closed regions from loose LINE/LWPOLYLINE/ARC segments (snap tol, planar-graph face walk, outer face dropped, holes kept). This is the missing reader feature: elevations drawn as line work yield closed wall/opening rectangles. Every loop lists its member handles.
- (c) **Grid alignment:** grid from column centres/`C L` lines per sheet; sheet-to-sheet transform from shared grid labels (the shed's `G3X/G3Y/OX/OY` constants become derived values with the residual reported).
- (d) **Units:** from `$INSUNITS` else stated assumption (listed in the defaults sheet, never silent).
**L7.2 Defaults = the existing L4 metadata editor, no new sheet (user 2026-10-18: "those sheets are the details/markers the user fills in, already in use; we merely highlight which are defaulted").** The editor's source badge (`you` / `from model` / `from file`) gets a fourth value `default`, drawn on a YELLOW background; a defaulted row is confirmed by editing it (it becomes `you`, as any file row does). The issues signal is the existing gate pill (blocked/needs-input), which shivers while any default stands. Feed: the `Defaults` register of dxf2prep (rows with `kind:'default'`). Grammar note: `@BY=` accepts model|user only (BIMCompiler verbatim copy), so a default is NOT written into the DXF: it exists only until confirmed, then it is written `@BY=user`. Built so far: the yellow `default` badge (dxf_meta_editor.js srcLabel + CSS); not yet: the importer emitting its fallbacks as `default` rows, the shiver.
**L7.3 Provenance colour.** Per body `prov = drawn | derived | assumed`. `assumed` = no drawing evidence for the body's EXISTENCE or POSITION (e.g. truss spacing, purlin count when not drawn): shown RED in 3D and listed in the sheet. A drawn body with a defaulted parameter (roof sheet thickness) is NOT red; the parameter is a yellow default in L7.2.
**L7.4 IFC typing (user 2026-10-18: "proper IFC element type metadata ... so 4D/5D can work later").** Each body carries `ifc_class`, `PredefinedType`, a type name taken from the drawing label when one exists (e.g. `12"x12" R.C.C Column`), `material` where stated, and `storey`. Mapping: column→IfcColumn/COLUMN, beam→IfcBeam/BEAM, footing→IfcFooting/PAD_FOOTING, grade beam→IfcBeam/BEAM (or IfcFooting/STRIP_FOOTING, decided by the drawing label), wall→IfcWall/STANDARD, roof sheet→IfcRoof→IfcSlab/ROOF (profile sheet), truss chords/webs/king post→IfcMember/CHORD|BRACE|POST, purlin→IfcMember/PURLIN, gusset→IfcPlate/GUSSET. No quantity or cost is invented: 5D needs quantities from the geometry (volume/area/length derived), unit rates are the user's.
**L7.5 Save and reopen.** Export the canvas as our `.db` (kernel op-log + elements with the L7.4 typing); the Viewer reopens it and the default 4D/5D JSONs (schedule by storey/discipline, quantity take-off) are generated for the film. Witness: reopen == saved element count, classes, volumes.
**L7.6 Film beats** (after the shed is complete): raise the roof (roof z offset gesture), extend a wall, hover on an element (what the hover shows is to be checked before it is filmed).
**L7.7 Trusses (shed).** Members from the section sheet's `Fo` layer (chords, king post, verticals, diagonals, purlin cross-sections, gussets), drawn evidence. NOT in the drawing: truss spacing along the length (one section line marked on the plan, at a location that is no column line). Built at every column line as `assumed` (RED) and listed as a default; owner to confirm.
**Witnesses (each names the issue it proves).** W-PREP-ID (duplicate guid is caught; the shed gable hatch yields 3 distinct ids), W-PREP-LOOPS (line-work wall rectangles of the shed elevations are recovered, member handles listed; control: removing one line breaks exactly one loop), W-PREP-DEFAULTS (every non-drawn value appears in the sheet; control: a stated value is not listed), W-IFC-TYPE (each body has class + predefined type; unknown mapping fails loudly).
**Not claimed:** that every CAD set imports; the line-loop feature is proven on the shed and the Duplex first.

### L7.2 Results (2026-10-18)
Built in `dxf_meta_editor.js` / `dxf_import_ui.js`: "default" = a value the drawing does not state that the editor only SUGGESTS (the existing PR-* suggestions: units from the header, view/name from the title text, grid, levels, door schedule/tags). Pending suggestions are drawn on a YELLOW background (`.dm-pr`, `.dm-defrow`) and the Metadata button shivers (yellow ring, 3 shakes, repeats once after 8 s, off under prefers-reduced-motion) while any stands; it stops when the last one is confirmed. **W-DXF-ATTN** (`modeller/tests/witness_dxf_attention.js`, real clicks): A1 raw sheet 3 pending -> shiver; A1b rows yellow (rgba 245,217,10); A3 confirm all -> shiver gone, markings are `user`; A2 control (sheet that states them) -> no shiver. 6 PASS / 0 FAIL. Not run: `witness_dxf_meta_editor.js` (needs the local BIMCompiler sandbox outputs, absent here). 3D->2D fact (user's reminder): the exporter's markings are `@BY=model` ("from model", purple); what it cannot resolve is logged `§MARK_GAP` and left blank, NEVER filled; so exported sets carry no defaults. Defaults only arise on foreign CAD sets, and the reader still never defaults (a missing marking is an error); a suggestion is the only default.

### L7.2 / L7.4 / L7.5 corrections after the user's review (2026-10-18)
- **Defaults are APPLIED values**, marked yellow wherever shown (rows of `DxfImportUI.state.defaults`, produced by the pre-processor/importer): the editor has a "Defaults applied" section (yellow `default` badge; a change makes it `you`, kept as a number if it was one, `DxfMetaEditor.defaultChoices()` records it). The earlier un-applied PR-* suggestions stay yellow too. **The shiver is on the Metadata (sheets) button only while the editor is CLOSED** (the editor auto-opens on import and shows everything itself; closing with unconfirmed values starts the shiver, reopening stops it). The 3D->2D Export stage does not open the editor (it writes `@BY=model` markings and downloads); a round trip re-import opens it with nothing yellow. W-DXF-ATTN 11/11.
- **Save as DB = the usual path** (Export > IFC4, then IFC -> DB extractor, or Native .db); 4D/5D come from the Viewer's Time Machine on open; nothing new. What had to be fixed so the typing survives that path: (1) `bonsai_ifc.js` wrote Name = "<class> <op id>" and no PredefinedType: it now writes Name + ObjectType = the drawing's type label and PredefinedType (IFC4 enums for Column/Beam/Footing/Member/Plate/Roof/Wall) from `provenance.ifc`; (2) **`guid(n)` produced at most 64 distinct GlobalIds** (LCG low bits): the shed's 457 products collapsed to 212 in the extractor; now unique (n is encoded in the last 6 chars). Result on the shed: IFC 457 typed, extracted DB 457 elements: IfcColumn 64, IfcFooting 32, IfcBeam 12, IfcWall 27, IfcRoof 4, IfcMember 192 (chord 42, post 14, brace 112, purlin 24), IfcPlate 126; element names = drawing labels. Not exported: material (needs IfcMaterial + IfcRelAssociatesMaterial), quantities. W-IFC-GUID 4/4.

### L7.2 final shape (user 2026-10-18, "retain same sheet design")
- The downloaded `_marked.dxf` IS the settings sheet. 3D->2D: every value is extracted from the model and written into it (`@BY=model`, nothing yellow). 2D->3D: the SAME sheet design in the editor; the user fills it by hand, and where the drawing is silent the defaults are applied and shown yellow so accepting is one tick.
- One extra section on top, "All defaults": repeats every yellow item (sheet suggestions, door suggestions, applied defaults) in one list; per-row tick, or "Accept all". Ignoring it, and the shiver, is a valid path: the defaults stay applied, nothing there gates Convert (the gate is the verdict only), the DB can be saved for a POC trial. W-DXF-ATTN 15/15 (Y1 list = every yellow once, Y1b one tick = one row, Y3 ignore path: shiver on, verdict unchanged by the defaults, Y2 Accept all = 0 left, suggestions written as `user`).

### L7.8 Pre-processor emits the defaults it applies (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; user: "go ahead with pre-processor defaults emission")
**Rule.** The pre-processor never applies a value silently: every value it uses that the drawing/markings do not state becomes a `Defaults` row (`kind:'default'`, key, value, why, options). A user choice (`DxfMetaEditor.defaultChoices()`) is fed back and the pre-processor re-runs with it: a choice must CHANGE the result, or it is not a default (witness control).
**Producer 1 (this slice): line-work loop detection.** `Dxf2Prep.prepare(entities, choices)`: for a LINE-WORK sheet (no closed polylines on non-marking layers, >= 8 LINEs; CAD elevations such as the shed's) it extracts segments (LINE, LWPOLYLINE edges; not `DXF2BIM-MARK`), runs `lineLoops` and returns `{lineWork, segments, loops, dropped, defaults}`. Applied default: `loops.snap_tol` = 0.01 drawing units (why: "line ends closer than this are joined; the drawing does not state a tolerance"; options 0.001, 0.01, 0.1). A sheet that is not line work emits NOTHING (no yellow noise on the Duplex or any std set).
**Wiring.** `dxf_import_ui.js` runs `prepare` per opened sheet after reading, stores `state.prep = {sheet: result}` and `state.defaults = rows`; the editor's "Defaults applied" / "All defaults" sections show them (already built); a change re-runs `prepare` with the choice and re-renders. The metadata panel shows "closed regions found: N" per line-work sheet (informational: the READER does not consume loops yet; stated, not claimed).
**Not in this slice:** the reader consuming loops as wall rectangles (next, its own spec), other producers (units/grid/thickness defaults for CAD bodies).
**Witnesses.** W-PREP-EMIT (Node): P1 a line-work sheet emits exactly one `loops.snap_tol` default; P2 a sheet with closed polylines emits none; P3 CONTROL a user choice changes the loop count (a 0.5-unit gap closes at tol 0.1, stays open at 0.001); P4 choice recorded as `chosen`. W-DXF-ATTN (browser): E1 opening a line-work DXF fills `state.defaults`, the row is yellow in the editor and in "All defaults", the Metadata button shivers when closed; E2 choosing another tolerance re-runs (log line + loop count changes); E3 opening a closed-polyline sheet leaves `state.defaults` empty.

#### L7.8 Results (2026-10-18)
Built as specified: `Dxf2Prep.prepare/isLineWork/segmentsOf` (dxf2prep.js), `DxfImportUI.reprepare` + `state.prep` + `state.defaults` (dxf_import_ui.js, `§DXF-PREP` log), the editor feeds a choice back (`refeed`) and shows "closed regions found: N" per line-work sheet; `sw.js` precaches dxf2prep.js (CACHE_VERSION not bumped, deploy time). **W-PREP-EMIT** (in witness_dxf_prep.js) EM1-EM7 PASS: one default per line-work set (shared across sheets), a closed-polyline sheet emits none, CONTROL tol 0.1 closes the open corner (2 regions) while 0.001 keeps it open (1), choice recorded `chosen`. **W-DXF-ATTN 16/16**: real import of a line-work DXF fills `state.defaults` (yellow in "Defaults applied" and "All defaults"), shiver when closed, choosing 0.1 re-runs the pre-processor (1 -> 2 regions, badge `you`), Accept all, non-line-work -> none. Not yet: the READER consuming the loops as wall rectangles (the CAD line-loop reader feature, its own spec); the shed's body-level assumptions (roof thickness, truss spacing, gradebeam top) still live in `shed_scene.js` `DEF` rows, not in the pre-processor.

### L7.9 Spec: the reader consumes line-work loops as elevation wall rectangles (2026-10-18, BEFORE code; "continue" after L7.8)
**Problem (shed finding).** A CAD elevation is loose lines; the reader's `elevWallRects` (dxf2elev.js, verbatim copy, NOT edited) wants closed outlines on an `elevwall` layer, so the gate found "no closed wall rectangles".
**Rule.** In `readSheet` (bim-ootb-owned `dxf2bim_import.js`), for a sheet whose markings say `@VIEW=ELEV` (or SECTION) and that is line work (`Dxf2Prep.isLineWork`): run `lineLoops` at the snap tolerance (default 0.01 drawing units, the L7.8 default row; a user choice overrides) and APPEND one closed 4-vertex LWPOLYLINE entity per AXIS-ALIGNED rectangular loop (corners within tol) with `synthetic:true`, `handle:'loop-<n>'`, `members:[line handles]` and the layer shared by ALL its member lines (mixed layers -> not synthesised, counted `mixed`). Non-rectangular loops (gables, triangles) are not synthesised, counted `nonRect`. Because the entity sits on the member layer, the user's existing `@LAYER` marking (the Layers section) decides which layers are walls: nothing here says what a loop IS.
**No change to what is not line work.** Sheets that are not ELEV/SECTION or not line work are byte-for-byte as before (std sets, Duplex: same entities, same `sha`). A line-work sheet's `sha` = hash(text + `|loops:<tol>`), so the run identity changes with the tolerance (same text, different tol, different result must not share a run_id).
**Defaults.** The `loops.snap_tol` default row is emitted ONLY for sheets where synthesis applied (not for every line-work sheet): the L7.8 emission moves from "any line-work sheet" to "a sheet the reader used it on". The editor passes its choices into `readSheet` (cache key includes them).
**Witnesses (Node, W-LOOPS-READER).** R1 a line-work ELEV drawn as 3 rectangles from 12 lines: before 0 rects through `elevWallRects` (layer mapped elevwall), after 3, positions equal the drawn corners (1e-9). R2 CONTROL an open corner (gap 0.05): 2 rects at tol 0.01, 3 at tol 0.1. R3 mixed-layer loop not synthesised, counted. R4 a gable triangle not synthesised, counted nonRect. R5 same sheet with `@VIEW=PLAN`: entities unchanged. R6 a std-style sheet with closed polylines: entities and sha unchanged. R7 sha differs between tol 0.01 and 0.1. Browser (W-DXF-ATTN): marking the line-work sheet `@VIEW=ELEV` in the editor makes the default row appear (and not before); choosing 0.1 changes the rect count.
**Not claimed:** the shed passing the gate (it also lacks a roof plan and level datums), only that its elevation rectangles are no longer missing.

#### L7.9 Results (2026-10-18) — and two corrections to the spec above found while building
- **Trigger changed (finding):** the spec's "sheet is line work" test is wrong for real CAD: the shed's elevations carry closed polylines on OTHER layers (rods), so `isLineWork` said "no" on all four. Replaced by the user's own typing: synthesis runs per LAYER the user typed `elevwall`/`sectwall` (`@LAYER=<layer>,elevwall`) on a sheet marked `@VIEW=ELEV|SECTION`, and only when that layer has >= 4 loose LINEs and no closed outline of its own. Nothing is synthesised until the user types the layer; std sets (layers typed by the set maps, closed outlines) are untouched, same entities, same `sha`. The L7.8 default row is emitted exactly when this applied.
- **Reader weakness found (not edited, verbatim copy):** `elevWallRects` reads ANY entity with vertices on an elevwall layer, so each loose LINE becomes a zero-area (or, for a sloped line, a bounding-box) rectangle. The synthesised outline therefore REPLACES its member LINEs (consumed by index; DXF handles are not always present or unique: the editor's mark TEXT can share a LINE's handle, which first broke the layer lookup). Sloped lines of non-rectangular loops stay as drawn (still bbox noise, pre-existing).
- **W-LOOPS-READER** (`modeller/tests/witness_dxf_loops_reader.js`) R0-R12 PASS: loose lines alone = 12 zero-area rects, 0 with area; marked + layer typed -> exactly 3 rectangles at the drawn corners (1e-9); CONTROL open corner 2 at tol 0.01, 3 at 0.1; both-layers-typed mixed loop counted, one-layer-typed side on another layer cannot close (nothing invented); gable triangle counted nonRect; PLAN view untouched; closed-outline sheet untouched; sha differs with tolerance; no module -> reader as before; no layer typed -> nothing; only the typed layer is read. **W-DXF-ATTN 17/17** (the default row appears only after `@VIEW=ELEV` on a sheet whose layer is typed; choosing 0.1 turns 1 rectangle into 2).
- **Real CAD (the shed's South elevation, local):** typing layer `0` as elevwall yields 25 rectangles (68 loose lines, 0 nonRect, 0 mixed), e.g. 36x98, 74x98, 110x98 in (bays between columns, 98 in tall: the section-line height, the 2 in discrepancy already on the owner list). West/East/North elevations carry their lines on other layers: the user types those layers.
- **Still not claimed:** the shed passing the gate (roof plan, level datums, plan wall pairs), and which rectangles are walls vs columns/beams (the user's layer typing and later a classification step).

#### L7.9 Gate probe on the real shed (2026-10-18, diagnostic; local script `ShedDrawing/tools/gate_probe.js`)
Floor plan + South/West/East elevations marked from the drawing (`marks.json`), the elevation wall layer typed (`@LAYER=0,elevwall`), set stated STD, plus a STUB roof sheet (the floor plan re-marked `@VIEW=ROOF`, `@ROOF=3.3`, a diagnostic placeholder, NOT a model). Result: the gate PASSES (roles, set) and the verdict is `blocked` with 47 items: `QF:layer_unmapped` 25 (layers the user has not typed yet), `QF:dim_text_mismatch` 7 (the drawing's own "410'-0\"" dimension text measures 39.624 m: a drawing fact for the owner), `QF:elev_level_mismatch` 3, `QF:elev_roof_unstated` 3, `QF:set_storey_unstated` 2, `QF:face_no_elev` 1 (North not in the set), `GAP:roof_no_outline/no_top/no_bottom` (no roof plan drawn), `GAP:slab_no_top` + `slab_no_exterior_wall` (an open-sided shed has no slab or exterior walls: honest gaps), `QF:check_skipped` (plan walls are 5 in pairs, not footprints). Before §L7.9 the gate refused earlier ("no closed wall rectangles"). Reading: the generic path now JUDGES the shed; most items are "user states" (layer typing, levels) or genuine drawing facts. The wall/slab/roof pipeline is not the right route for the shed's columns, beams, footings, trusses: those need typed bodies from loops (next spec, §L7.10: a user-typed layer -> body kind -> IFC typing; extrusion from the plan/elevation pair, thickness/height from drawn dimensions, every non-drawn value a yellow default).

## L7.10 Typed bodies from drawn loops (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; "proceed" after the shed gate probe)
**Why.** The wall/slab/roof pipeline cannot express a shed's columns, beams, footings, trusses; `shed_scene.js` builds them by hand. The generic version: the USER types layers with a body kind; the extrusion comes from drawn geometry; whatever is not drawn is a gap or a yellow default; every body carries IFC typing (§L7.4) and provenance (§L7.3).
**Grammar (BIMCompiler `dxf2marks.js`, then `sync.sh`; the copies here are verbatim).** `@LAYER=<layer>,<kind>` with `kind` in the new `LAYER_TYPES.BODY` list: `column`, `beam`, `footing`, `gradebeam`, `member`, `plate`. Valid on ANY view (like `dims`/`grid`); `layerMap` maps them to `ignore`, so every existing reader skips them (no behaviour change for existing sets). Witness BIMCompiler side: a `@LAYER=Col,column` marking is accepted on PLAN and ELEV, `@LAYER=Col,flux` is still `MARK:layer_type`, existing sets' layer maps unchanged.
**Module `modeller/dxf2bodies.js` (bim-ootb owned, pure, Node + browser).** `buildBodies(sheets, opts)` -> `{ els, gaps, defaults, report }`:
1. Per sheet, the typed body layers from `desc.layers`. Regions = closed outlines on that layer (LWPOLYLINE shape, or §L7.9 loops from loose LINEs at the snap tolerance) that are axis-aligned rectangles; others counted `nonRect`.
2. PLAN regions give the footprint (x, y; metres by the sheet's units). ELEVATION regions of the same kind (through the existing `MK.align` map: horizontal -> plan coordinate, vertical -> z metres) give the z range.
3. Match: a plan region meets the elevation regions whose horizontal interval along that face's axis equals the plan interval (tolerance = the elevation tolerance of the config). All matching regions across the elevations that give ONE distinct (z0, z1) -> body z range, `height_source:'elevation'`, `prov:'drawn'`. Several distinct ranges -> `GAP:body_ambiguous_z` (listed, no body). None -> `GAP:body_no_elevation` (listed, no body; never a default z: a missing height is the user's to state). Elevation-only regions (no plan region) are not bodies.
4. Each body: footprint extruded z0..z1 (`Dxf2Shell.prismRect`), `class` + `PredefinedType` from the kind via `Dxf2Prep.ifcType` (column->IfcColumn/COLUMN, beam->IfcBeam/BEAM, footing->IfcFooting/PAD_FOOTING, gradebeam->IfcBeam/BEAM, member->IfcMember/MEMBER, plate->IfcPlate/PLATE), type name = the drawing's label text nearest inside/at the region when exactly one exists else null (listed as a yellow default `<kind>.type_name`), unique id `<handle>` (§L7.1), provenance record with the member handles.
5. Output is the importer's own `els` form, so `toOps` / IFC export / DB extraction are unchanged.
**Not in this slice:** feeding the bodies into `convert` (the gate's verdict is about walls/slabs/roof; a bodies-only/structure set needs its own gate rule, next spec), footings/grade beams below the lowest elevation datum (their z is in a details section: `@VIEW=SECTION` regions, next), trusses.
**Witnesses (W-BODIES, Node).** B1 synthetic plan (3 column rectangles) + a South elevation (3 column rectangles at the same intervals, plus a bay panel that matches nothing) -> exactly 3 columns, z range = the drawn one, class IfcColumn/COLUMN; B2 CONTROL the elevation panel is not a body; B3 CONTROL a column with no elevation counterpart -> `GAP:body_no_elevation`, no body; B4 CONTROL two elevations giving different heights -> `GAP:body_ambiguous_z`; B5 FALSIFIER an untyped layer produces nothing; B6 ids unique; B7 mesh volume == footprint area x height (1e-9) and every triangle winding outward; B8 same input twice -> identical output (determinism). Real CAD (local shed): columns from `0_FLOOR_PLAN` (layer `Col`) x South elevation (layer `0`) == the 32 hand-traced columns' footprints (12 x 12 in) with z from the drawing, listed with the rest as gaps; report counts only.

#### L7.10 Results (2026-10-18)
Built: BIMCompiler grammar `LAYER_TYPES.BODY` (PR red1oon/BIMCompiler#209, test LM8), `modeller/dxf2bodies.js` (`buildBodies`), `Dxf2Prep.ifcType` gains `member`/`plate`. **W-BODIES** (`modeller/tests/witness_dxf_bodies.js`) B1-B9 PASS: plan footprints x South elevation -> 3 columns, z 0..3 m as drawn, IfcColumn/COLUMN, volume == 0.3 x 0.3 x 3 (1e-9), outward winding; CONTROL bay panel matches nothing; CONTROL no elevation counterpart -> GAP:body_no_elevation; CONTROL South 3.0 vs North 3.5 -> GAP:body_ambiguous_z x3 (no body); FALSIFIER untyped layer -> nothing; deterministic; beam kind -> IfcBeam/BEAM.
Two additions found on real CAD (not in the spec above): (1) **line pairs**: a CAD elevation draws a column as its two vertical faces (no top/bottom line), so two neighbouring vertical lines on a typed layer, same height, closer than `bodies.pair_max_width` (default 0.5 m, a yellow default row, option 0.25/0.5/1) are read as one region on the ELEVATION side; (2) **centre match**: when no elevation interval equals the plan interval, a region on the same centre line (|dc| <= tol) with width within 50% is accepted and the body is marked `match:'centre'` (footprint from the plan, reported in `report.centre`, never silent).
**Real shed (local probe `ShedDrawing/tools/bodies_probe.js`, report only):** plan layer `Col` x South elevation layer `0` typed column: 32 plan columns, strict tolerance (3 mm): 0 bodies, 32 `GAP:body_no_elevation`. Diagnosis: the elevation's drawn column pairs are 10 in apart (plan columns 12 in) and their centres sit ~9 in off the plan grid because the hand-derived `@GRIDH` marks were placed at the label text insertion points; with a deliberately loose 0.3 m tolerance (diagnostic only) 22 of 32 would match, but at z -108..-10 in and -108..-72 in relative to the datum the marks state (`@Z=0` at y=-4338.6), i.e. BELOW the datum: the datum/gridline marks of that elevation need the owner/user, so the generic path correctly refuses to build rather than guess. Not claimed: the shed's columns through this path.
**Not in this slice (unchanged):** feeding bodies into `convert` (a structure-only gate rule), footings/grade beams (z from a details section), trusses; type names from drawing labels (spec item 4: not built, `type_name` stays null).

#### L7.10 follow-up: the shed's South elevation marks restated from evidence (2026-10-18; local `ShedDrawing/tools/marks_fix_south.py`)
The 0-of-32 result was the marks, not the module. Evidence in the drawing: (1) the dimension chain's extension ticks (layer `Dim`, short verticals above the grid labels) sit at the column-pair centres (-24685, -24565, ...); the grid label TEXT is inserted ~9-10 in left of its tick, so the earlier label-insertion marks were 9-10 in off. (2) The datum: the heavy ground line (longest horizontal on layer 0, lowest, y = -4470.6) is z 0 (FGL); the horizontal the `PL` label sits on is exactly 24.0 in above it (y = -4446.6; the drawing states PL = 2'-0" above FGL); the earlier mark `@Z=0` at y = -4338.6 was the BEAM SOFFIT/top (the 96-in band above it is the truss), which is why the column pairs looked "below the datum". (3) Column pairs run y -4446.6..-4348.6 = PL..PL+98 = z 24..122 in; the 10-in band above (-4348.6..-4338.6) is the beam (B-1 12"x10"); the 122 in is the "2 in difference" (120 dimension vs 122 section lines) already on the owner list: now seen in the elevation too.
**Result at STRICT tolerance (3 mm, no loosening):** 24 of 32 plan columns matched by centre line (drawn pair 10 in, plan 12 in, `match:'centre'`), 8 `GAP:body_no_elevation` (grid 38 columns: their pair is offset 10 in from the tick, not matched); heights: 12 at z 24..122 in, 12 at z 24..60 in (right block: the elevation draws those columns only 36 in tall from PL; hidden behind the wall or truly short is not decidable from one elevation: a second elevation would surface it as `GAP:body_ambiguous_z`, not guess). The hand-built model had these columns from FGL (z 0) to 120: the generic path says PL..122 and leaves 0..24 (plinth/stub) to the details sheet.
**Generic lesson -> next spec (§L7.11):** the editor's grid proposal reads label text; CAD sets whose dimension chains have extension ticks give the TRUE grid position, and the datum is the ground line, not the lowest dimension. Propose `PR-GRIDH` from ticks when a label has a unique tick within 15 in (reported with the offset), and `PR-Z` from the ground line + a label ("PL", "FGL", "+0.000") when one exists; both stay suggestions (yellow), never silent.

## L7.12 Typed bodies in the Modeller UI: Layers choices + structure-set Convert (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; "proceed with items 1 and 2")
**1. Layers section offers the BODY kinds.** The editor's Layers select (per role: plan, elev, roof, section) gains Column, Beam, Footing, Grade beam, Member, Plate (the `@LAYER=<layer>,<kind>` types of BIMCompiler 434ffdec, valid on any view). `ruleType`/`uiOf` are the identity for these; `uiChoices` accepts `LAYER_TYPES.BODY`. A chosen kind is a user `@LAYER` marking like any other (written into the downloaded `_marked.dxf`).
**2. Structure set.** A set is a STRUCTURE set when any sheet states a body kind. For it `convertGate` does not run the wall/slab/roof pipeline; it runs `Dxf2Bodies.buildBodies` and judges: roles (every sheet has a role from its markings, >= 1 plan, >= 1 elevation) else refused exactly as the standard gate; `qualifies` when >= 1 body is built, else blocked (`QF:no_bodies`); every `GAP:body_*` is ADVISORY (listed, those columns are left out, never defaulted); **mixed sets** (a body kind AND a wall/slab/opening/roof layer typed) are refused with the reason "mixed sets are not supported yet" (stated, not silent). The result `R` is the importer's own form (`els`, `ST`, `run_id` = hash of the sheet shas + the tolerance + `structure`), so Convert/`toOps`/IFC export/DB extraction are unchanged: bodies land on the canvas as GEOM_INSERT with `provenance.ifc` (class, PredefinedType) and `prov_kind`. The panel says "Structure set: N bodies, M left out". Sets without a body kind take the unchanged path (std sets, Duplex: same code path, same results).
**Witness W-DXF-STRUCT (browser, real clicks):** S1 a synthetic plan + South elevation (marks present, layer `Col` untyped): the Layers select for `Col` offers `column` on both sheets (and not on a role-less sheet); S2 choose Column on both -> structure set: verdict qualifies, Convert enabled, 0 blocking, 0 advisory; S3 click Convert -> op-log holds exactly 3 GEOM_INSERT IfcColumn, bbox z = the drawn range, `provenance.ifc.PredefinedType` COLUMN; S4 CONTROL a 4th plan column with no elevation counterpart -> 1 advisory GAP:body_no_elevation, still qualifies, Convert builds 3 (not 4); S5 CONTROL untyped layer -> the standard gate path (no structure verdict); S6 CONTROL a wall-typed layer next to a body kind -> refused "mixed sets"; S7 the saved `_marked.dxf` of the structure set re-imports to the same bodies (round trip). Node: existing W-LOOPS-READER/W-BODIES/W-PREP unchanged.
**Not in this slice:** items 3 (editor proposals from dimension ticks / ground line, §L7.11), mixed walls + bodies, footings/grade beams heights, trusses, type names from labels.

#### L7.12 Results (2026-10-18)
Built as specified: the editor's Layers select offers column, beam, footing, grade beam, member, plate (`dxf_meta_editor.js`: `BODY_KINDS`, `uiOf`, `uiChoices`); `dxf_import_ui.js` `structureGate()` (a set that states a body kind is a STRUCTURE set: roles judged as in the standard gate, `Dxf2Bodies.buildBodies`, `GAP:body_*` advisory, mixed sets refused, `by_shape` zeros for the editor table, `set: null` so the editor keeps its default layer-set maps), `dxf2bodies.js` loaded by the import UI and precached by `sw.js` (CACHE_VERSION not bumped, deploy time). **W-DXF-STRUCT 8/8** (real clicks, synthetic DXF): S1 body kinds offered on plan + elevation; S5 untyped -> standard gate (no structure verdict); S2 typed -> qualifies, 3 bodies, 0 blocking/advisory, Convert enabled; S3 Convert -> 3 IfcColumn on Level 1, 0 cuts, `provenance.ifc` IfcColumn/COLUMN, z height 3 m; S7 the marked sheets re-open to the same 3 bodies; S4 4th column with no elevation -> 1 advisory, still 3 built (left out, never defaulted); S6 wall layer next to a body kind -> refused "mixed sets are not supported yet". W-DXF-ATTN 17/17, W-BODIES, W-PREP, W-LOOPS-READER, W-IFC-GUID unchanged PASS. Not run here: `witness_dxf_meta_editor.js`/`witness_dxf_import_node.js` (local BIMCompiler sandbox outputs). Not in this slice: §L7.11 editor proposals (grid from dimension ticks, datum from the ground line), mixed walls + bodies, footing/grade-beam heights, trusses, type names from labels.
