That DB came from the scripted trace, not yet from a real import: with L7.12 the columns can now come through the product; beams/footings/trusses/roof remain scripted.

## L7.11 Editor suggestions from CAD evidence: grid from dimension ticks, datum from the ground line (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; "proceed till all done")
**Why.** The editor's grid proposal (PR-GRID) needs lines on a grid layer plus a one-character label; CAD sets (the shed) have neither: grid labels sit next to the dimension chain's extension TICKS, and the label text is inserted ~0.25 m off the grid position. Hand-placed label positions made 0 of 32 columns match (§L7.10 follow-up). The datum is the heavy GROUND line, not the lowest dimension.
**PR-GRID-TICK** (key GRIDX / GRIDY on PLAN, GRIDH on ELEV/SECTION; only for labels the strict PR-GRID did not propose): a label is a TEXT/MTEXT `^[A-Z]$|^\d{1,3}$`. Its tick = a SHORT axis-aligned LINE (<= 1.0 m, perpendicular to the grid direction) with exactly ONE such tick within 0.5 m (along) of the label's insertion point and an end within 1.0 m (across). Zero or several candidates -> refused with the reason (never a guess); a tick claimed by two labels -> both refused. Proposal value = the tick position (3 decimals), `why` names the tick handle and the label-to-tick offset. Yellow, a suggestion until ticked.
**PR-Z-GROUND** (ELEV/SECTION, only when PR-Z found nothing): the longest horizontal LINE at the LOWEST y that spans >= 80% of the sheet's horizontal extent is the ground line -> `@Z=0` at that y (why: "ground line"). The scale is known from @UNITS; a second @Z (the grammar wants 2, as a consistency check) is NOT invented: it stays a red "needed" row for the user, or comes from PR-Z.
**Witnesses (browser, real clicks) W-DXF-TICKS.** T1 a CAD-style elevation (labels 10 units left of their ticks, no grid layer, ground line, no level texts): suggestions PR-GRID-TICK x2 at the TICK positions (not the label positions) + PR-Z-GROUND, yellow; T2 ticking them writes `@GRIDH=..`@tick x, `@Z=0`@ground y as `user` markings and the elevation then aligns with the plan (no `MARK:align_fail`; the aligned map sends the tick x to the plan grid position, residual < 1 mm); T3 CONTROL a label with no tick within 0.5 m -> not proposed, refusal listed; T4 CONTROL two ticks within 0.5 m of one label -> refused (ambiguous); T5 CONTROL a sheet that already has a grid layer + labels: the strict PR-GRID result is unchanged (no tick proposals duplicate it); T6 on the real shed South elevation (local): the 14 tick proposals equal the 14 hand-restated @GRIDH values to 1e-3 in and the ground proposal equals y -4470.6.
**Not in this slice:** the second @Z from a vertical dimension chain; plan-sheet ticks when the plan has a grid layer.

#### L7.11 Results (2026-10-18)
Built in `dxf_meta_editor.js`: `propGridTicks` (PR-GRID-TICK; plan GRIDX/GRIDY and elevation/section GRIDH) and `propZGround` (PR-Z-GROUND, only when PR-Z found nothing). Change from the spec text: the ground line may be drawn in SEGMENTS, so the rule is "the lines at one height together cover >= 80% of the sheet, the LOWEST such height" (a stray short dimension line below it, the shed's EC-pad dimension at 2%, is not the ground); a label with NO tick is silent (dimension texts and elevation numbers are not grid labels), only an AMBIGUOUS label (>1 tick within 0.5 m) is listed as refused. **W-DXF-TICKS 8/8** (`modeller/tests/witness_dxf_ticks.js`): T1 suggestions at the tick positions (1000 / 4000), not the label text (990 / 3990), + ground `@Z=0`, yellow; T2 ticking them aligns the elevation with the plan (1000 -> 1.000 m, 4000 -> 4.000 m, z 0 -> 0, y 3000 -> 3 m); T3 label C with no tick not proposed; T4 two ticks near one label -> refused; T5 typed grid layer: strict PR-GRID unchanged, no tick duplicates; T7 segmented ground + stray lower dimension line. **T6 real shed (local probe):** the editor, given the South elevation with NO grid and NO Z marks, proposes 14 PR-GRID-TICK values equal to the 14 hand-restated `@GRIDH` to 0.0000 in, and PR-Z-GROUND at y = -4470.581 (the hand-restated ground line). W-DXF-ATTN 17/17 and W-DXF-STRUCT 8/8 unchanged. Still the user's: the second `@Z` (the `PL` +24 in is stated in the column detail, not on the elevation).

## L7.13 Type names for bodies from the drawing's own labels (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code)
**Why.** A body built from loops carries IFC class + PredefinedType but no type name, so the Viewer/ERP list "IfcColumn 1 … $ 1,294" although the drawing says `12"x12" R.C.C Column`. The name is evidence the drawing already holds.
**Rule (`dxf2bodies.js`).** For each kind, collect the distinct label texts (TEXT/MTEXT with MTEXT codes stripped, whitespace collapsed, not on the marking layer) of ALL given sheets that contain the kind's word (column / beam / footing / grade beam / member / plate, case-insensitive, whole word). EXACTLY ONE distinct label -> it is applied as `ifc.type_name` of every body of that kind AS A YELLOW DEFAULT row `<kind>.type_name` (why: "the one label on the drawings that names this kind; the drawing does not link it to a particular body"), options = [label, ''] ('' = no name); the user's choice (`prepChoices`) wins. Zero or several distinct labels -> no name, nothing invented; the candidates are listed in `report.labels[kind]`. The name travels into the IFC (Name + ObjectType, already built) and so into the DB, Find and the ERP lines.
**Witnesses.** W-BODIES B10 one label -> applied + default row; B11 CONTROL two different labels -> no name; B12 choice '' -> no name, row `chosen`; B13 MTEXT codes stripped (`{\fArial|b0;12"x12" R.C.C Column}` -> `12"x12" R.C.C Column`); B14 a label for another kind does not name this kind; W-DXF-STRUCT S8 (UI): the label appears as a yellow default in "Defaults applied" and "All defaults", the converted insert carries it, choosing the empty option converts without a name. Real shed (W-DXF-SHED H6): the 24 columns carry `12"x12" R.C.C Column`.
#### L7.11/L7.12 end-to-end on the REAL shed, through the product (2026-10-18; `modeller/tests/witness_dxf_shed_columns.js`, W-DXF-SHED 6/6, SKIP when the local sheets are absent)
Two small editor improvements found by running the raw sheets: PR-VIEW reads mixed-case titles ("Shed Floor Plan", "South Elevation": the regex was upper-case only) and PR-FACE reads compass words (SOUTH = minY, NORTH = maxY, WEST = minX, EAST = maxX, "north up", a stated convention); W-DXF-TICKS T8/T9 (10/10 total). Flow with NO script: open the two raw sheets (plan + South elevation) -> only UNITS/VIEW/NAME suggested (H1) -> Accept all -> the plan and elevation grids come from the dimension ticks (14 elevation marks), @Z=0 from the ground line, FACE minY (H2) -> the user types SET, the plan LEVEL 0 and the second datum (PL +24 in = 0.6096 m at y -4446.6, the one fact the elevation does not state) (H3) -> Layers: Col -> Column on the plan, 0 -> Column on the elevation -> STRUCTURE set, 24 bodies, 8 left out (advisory) (H4) -> Convert: 24 IfcColumn on Level 1, 12 at 24..122 in above FGL and 12 drawn 36 in tall (H5). Frames: scratchpad `film/01..05_*.png` (no client text visible).
**Remaining shed items stay scripted and why:** beams / grade beams / footings sit on shared layers (`Dim` holds dimension lines AND beam edges) or need a typical-detail section for their heights; trusses/purlins/gussets are a typical section repeated at an UNSTATED spacing (assumed, red); roof panels need the roof outline the drawing does not give; doors, fans, EC pads need owner answers (heights/thickness not drawn). Each is a spec of its own; none is claimed here.

#### L7.13 Results (2026-10-18)
Built in `dxf2bodies.js` (`labelsFor`, `cleanText`, `names`); one distinct label naming the kind is applied as a yellow default row `<kind>.type_name` (options: the label, ''), the user's choice wins, zero/several labels invent nothing. Two fixes found on the way: the pair-width default row now appears only when a body was actually matched through a drawn line pair, and a line pair that is merely the two sides of an already closed region is not a second region. **W-BODIES B10-B16 PASS** (applied + default row; two labels -> none; empty choice -> none, row `chosen`; MTEXT codes stripped; another kind's label ignored; same label twice = one; no pair row for closed boxes). **W-DXF-STRUCT 11/11** (S8: the label is a yellow default in "Defaults applied" and "All defaults", the converted insert carries `provenance.ifc.type_name`, choosing the empty option converts without a name). **W-DXF-SHED 7/7** (H6: the real shed's 24 columns carry `12"x12" R.C.C Column`). W-DXF-ATTN 17/17, W-PREP/W-LOOPS-READER/W-IFC-GUID PASS. Film draft: `prompts/FILM_DXF2BIM_BRICSCAD.md`.

### State of the shed against the lane (2026-10-18, end of this stretch)
THROUGH THE PRODUCT, from raw sheets: units/view/name suggestions, grid from dimension ticks, ground-line datum, compass face, typed Column layers, structure-set Convert (24 columns, 8 left out), type names from the drawing's label, IFC typing, IFC -> DB, Viewer Find/cost/ERP. STILL SCRIPTED (each needs its own spec and, for most, an owner answer): beams and grade beams (shared `Dim` layer; heights need band regions and containment matching), footings (heights from a typical-detail section), trusses/purlins/gussets (typical section at an unstated spacing: assumed, red), roof panels (no roof outline drawn), gable-end walls, doors/fans/EC pads (heights/thickness not drawn), the East-end zigzag hatch. Owner questions open: roof sheet thickness, truss spacing and whether the right block repeats the left, support-wall height vs the 24 in text, the 2 in (120 vs 122), wall centring.

#### Gap closure: the 8 unmatched columns (2026-10-18)
Cause (measured, `ShedDrawing/tools/gaps_probe.js`): the 8 left-out columns were the END columns (grids 01 and 42, 4 each). CAD drew their two faces with UNEQUAL extents (grid 01: one face z 0..132 in down to the footing line, the other 24..122) and one face of grid 42 in two collinear pieces split at a datum, so the equal-extent pair rule found no region. Fix in `dxf2bodies.js` `pairRegions` (generic): collinear touching/overlapping vertical pieces on one x merge into one face first; two neighbouring faces form a region whose height is the OVERLAP of their extents when the overlap is >= 50% of the LONGER face (a face drawn longer, down to a footing line, does not stretch the body; a tiny face inside a long one is not a body). Result on the real shed: ALL 32 columns built, 0 left out: 16 at 24..122 in, 12 at 24..60 (right block), 4 at 24..120 (east end). Independent corroboration: adding the North elevation (read right-to-left: FACE maxY from "North", 14 tick marks, HAXIS -X, its own PL line y -3895.2) gives the SAME 32 bodies with the SAME heights, 0 `GAP:body_ambiguous_z`: the two faces agree, nothing was reconciled by guessing (W-DXF-SHED B1). W-BODIES B17-B20 (unequal faces take the overlap; collinear pieces merge; overlap < 50% is no body; a middle line is not a neighbour). The film's "8 left out" sentence is obsolete: 32 of 32.

## L7.14 Several plans, and a z range the user states (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; "finish the gaps if they can be done first")
**Why.** Footings and grade beams sit on the FOUNDATION plan, beams on the BEAM-LAYOUT plan: separate plan sheets of the same storey with their own drawing origin, and no elevation shows them (below grade / hidden). The reader and the structure gate so far take one plan and an elevation z range.
**1. Several plans in a structure set.** The base plan = the lowest `@LEVEL`, then the first by name; every other plan is aligned to it by SHARED grid labels (`@GRIDX`/`@GRIDY`), translation only: per axis the offsets `base[l]*k_base - this[l]*k_this` of the shared labels must agree within the align tolerance (1 mm) and each axis needs >= 1 shared label, else `GAP:body_plan_not_aligned` (that sheet's bodies are left out, listed). Regions of one kind found on several plans are deduplicated by footprint (1 mm): the first (base) plan wins, so a column drawn on the floor plan and the foundation plan is ONE body.
**2. `@BODYZ=<kind>,<z0 m>,<z1 m>`** (BIMCompiler #210, the user's statement for a kind no elevation shows; one range per kind, z0 < z1). A plan region of that kind WITHOUT elevation evidence takes the stated range: `height_source:'stated'`, `prov:'derived'` (position drawn, height stated by the user). A region WITH elevation evidence keeps the evidence (the stated range never overrides a drawn one). Two sheets stating different ranges for one kind -> `GAP:body_stated_z_conflict`, nothing built for that kind. No statement: the gap stays (never a default).
**3. Editor "Body heights" section** (same sheet design, yellow only for suggestions/defaults: this is the user's own statement): kinds typed on a plan that have regions without elevation evidence are listed with the count; two inputs z0 / z1 (m) + Add writes `@BODYZ` (user marking) on the plan sheet; a trash icon removes it; the verdict re-runs.
**Witnesses.** W-BODIES: B21 a second plan (different origin) aligned by shared labels: its footings land in the base frame (centre equals the expected metres); B22 CONTROL labels disagree by > 1 mm -> `GAP:body_plan_not_aligned`, none of its bodies; B23 the same column on two plans -> one body; B24 `@BODYZ` gives the missing range (`height_source:'stated'`, prov derived); B25 CONTROL a region with elevation evidence keeps it; B26 CONTROL no statement -> `GAP:body_no_elevation` stays; B27 two sheets, two ranges -> `GAP:body_stated_z_conflict`. W-DXF-STRUCT S9 (UI): a second plan with footings, the section lists them, typing the range builds them, removing it leaves the gaps. The base frame is that of the FIRST plan by name (the floor plan sorts before the beam and foundation plans); the other plans are expressed in it. Real shed (W-DXF-SHED F1-F3): the foundation plan typed footing + column: grid from ticks (plan), 32 footings at the typed range, columns dedupe with the floor plan's (still 32 columns).
**Not in this slice:** beams / grade beams (their lines are drawn broken at every column and in different pieces on the two edges: bridging that is not stated by the drawing), trusses, roof, doors, fans, pads.

#### L7.14 Results (2026-10-18)
Built: BIMCompiler grammar `@BODYZ` (PR #210, test LM9), `dxf2bodies.js` (plan alignment by shared grid labels with `GAP:body_plan_not_aligned`, footprint dedupe across plans, stated z range with conflict gap, several plans of one storey = ONE storey), editor section "Body heights" (kinds waiting, two inputs, Add/trash), structure gate ST by distinct level. **W-BODIES B21-B27 PASS**, **W-DXF-STRUCT 15/15** (S9: second plan with footings listed as waiting, typing the range builds them in the BASE frame, Convert -> 3 IfcColumn + 2 IfcFooting on Level 1, removing the statement puts them back to waiting). **Real shed W-DXF-SHED 11/11, flow C:** floor plan + foundation plan + South elevation raw -> the foundation plan's grid comes from its ticks too (14 + 4 labels, F1) -> 32 columns built (the foundation plan's `Col` columns dedupe with the floor plan's), the 32 `Fo` footings wait (F2) -> the user types the range from the footing detail (-1.4478..-1.143 m = FGL -57..-45 in: 3" blinding + 12" footing under the 45" column in the ground) -> 32 footings 48 x 60 in as drawn (4'-0" x 5'-0", F-1), EACH centred on a floor-plan column (F3): the two plans align through their shared grid labels. The base frame is that of the first plan by name. NOT done and why (unchanged): beams and grade beams are drawn as two parallel lines broken at every column and in different pieces on the two edges (bridging them is a rule the drawing does not state), trusses/purlins/gussets need a spacing the drawing does not state, roof/gable-end walls/doors/fans/EC pads need owner answers (heights, thickness, outline).

## L8 Abstraction: pure modules + a headless runner (spec = the RESUME Q0 order of 2026-10-18; built and witnessed 2026-10-18)
**Rule.** Logic lives in PURE modules (UMD, Node + browser, no `window`, no editor state); the Modeller pages are thin adapters; every rule is witnessed in Node. **Extracted (behaviour-preserving):** `modeller/dxf2suggest.js` (all PR-* rules: UNITS/VIEW/NAME/SET/FACE/HAXIS/GRID/GRID-TICK/Z/Z-GROUND, plus `stripMarks`, `markText`, `effText`), `modeller/dxf2structure.js` (`isStructure`, `judge`: the structure-set judgement), both injected with their context (`create(ctx)`, `judge(sheets, {I, B, choices})`); `dxf_meta_editor.js` / `dxf_import_ui.js` keep only the wiring (`SUG()`, `structureGate()`), loaded via the import UI script list + `sw.js`. **New:** `modeller/dxf2ifc.js` (the typed-body IFC4 writer, web-ifc injected like `ifc_extract_core.js`) and `modeller/dxf2bim_cli.js` (`--sheets ... --accept-suggestions --set --level --datum --haxis --layer role:layer=kind --bodyz kind=z0,z1 --choice k=v --ifc --out`; writes the marked settings sheets, `verdict.json`, `bodies.json`, `ops.json`, `model.ifc`; exit 0 qualifies / 2 does not / 1 usage error). The CLI replicates the page's gestures one for one (Accept all = fixpoint over the PR-* proposals; typed facts = the page's fields), so the marked sheets it writes ARE the whole configuration: feeding them back with no flags gives the same bodies (C5).
**Witnesses.** W-PURE (Node, `witness_dxf_pure.js`, SG1-SG9 suggestion rules incl. the tick/ground/segment/ambiguity/typed-grid/title/face controls, ST1-ST7 structure judgement incl. mixed refusal, no plan, falsifier, determinism) PASS; W-CLI (`witness_dxf_cli.js`, C1-C14: raw synthetic sheets + flags -> 3 IfcColumn, marked sheets + verdict + ops written, round trip without flags, controls: no layer typed -> exit 2, mixed -> refused, unknown flag -> exit 1, datum naming no sheet -> exit 1, `--ifc` model.ifc typed + the usual extractor reads it `elements=3`; REAL raw shed C10-C12: 32 IfcColumn + 32 IfcFooting, 0 left out, heights 16 at 24..122 in / 12 at 24..60 / 4 at 24..120, footings 48 x 60 in at the stated range each centred on a column, type name `12"x12" R.C.C Column`: the same numbers as W-DXF-SHED, no browser) PASS; **PARITY** W-DXF-STRUCT S10: the page's IFC (`Bonsai.ifc.build()`) and the headless IFC (dxf2ifc.js over the SAME effective sheets) have the same products (class, Name, ObjectType, PredefinedType), the same storey and the same vertices to 3 mm (float32 vs float64): the two writers are one model. Browser witnesses unchanged: W-DXF-ATTN 17, -STRUCT 16, -TICKS 10, -SHED 11. Real shed through the CLI to the DB: `--ifc` then `extractIFC2DB.js` -> IfcColumn 32 + IfcFooting 32.
**Not yet abstracted:** `doorProposals` (door schedule/tag suggestions) and the standard (wall/slab/roof) gate wiring stay in the page; `bonsai_ifc.js` (op-log -> IFC for the Modeller's own authored geometry) keeps its own writer (W-DXF-STRUCT S10 keeps the two in step). Next: Q1 film v2.

## L9 Beams and grade beams from drawn bands (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; RESUME queue Q2)
**Evidence (shed, measured).** Beam layout plan (sheet 3, layer `Dim`; the same layer holds dimension lines but its parallel LINE pairs are only beams): a long beam = two parallel horizontal lines 12 in apart; one edge is drawn as ONE long line, the other in PIECES between the columns (the columns hide it); end beams = vertical pairs, 188 in long between the column rows; the EXPANSION JOINT = a vertical pair 8.7 in apart (two pieces). The South elevation draws the beam as a horizontal BAND: a long top line (z 132 in) and soffit pieces (z 122 in). Both elevation blocks are drawn 10 in deep although the label says `B-2 12"x12"` for the right block: a drawing conflict to SURFACE, not reconcile.
**Rule.**
1. **Band regions** (`dxf2bodies.js`, kinds `beam`, `gradebeam`, `member`; both orientations; plan AND elevation): two neighbouring parallel axis-aligned LINEs on a typed layer closer than `bodies.pair_max_width` (the existing yellow default, 0.5 m) bound a band over the OVERLAP of their extents. A line may bound several bands (one long edge + the pieces of the other edge: each piece is a region = the span between column faces: NO bridging across columns); the shorter edge must lie within the longer edge's extent (tol) or overlap it by >= 50% of the longer (the column rule); collinear touching pieces merge first (as for column faces).
2. **Heights**: ELEVATION bands (horizontal pair: soffit/top) of the same kind give z via the aligned map, matched to the plan region by interval along the elevation's axis (interval equal, else centre line with width within 50%: reported `match`); else `@BODYZ`; else the gap stays. End beams are matched by the West/East elevations when those are typed.
3. **Exclusion**: regions the user does not want (the expansion joint) are excluded with the EXISTING `@EXCLUDE=x0,y0,x1,y1` marking (the editor's Exclude tool; CLI `--exclude <sheet>:x0,y0,x1,y1`): a plan region whose rectangle lies inside an exclude rectangle (drawing units, on that sheet) is skipped and COUNTED (`report.excluded`). The module does not decide what a joint is.
4. **Type name**: a label `[A-Z]{1,3}-\d+` (e.g. `B-1`) whose insertion point lies within 0.8 m of the region and is the ONLY such label within that distance names the region (`ifc.type_name`, yellow default row per distinct name); sheet titles (text ending PLAN/ELEVATION/SECTION) never name a kind (the `Beam Lay out Plan` title must not become the beams' type name: fix `labelsFor`).
5. **Provenance/IFC**: `IfcBeam/BEAM` (gradebeam: IfcBeam/BEAM; footing-level grade beams keep their own typed layer), `prov` drawn when z comes from an elevation, derived when stated.
**Witnesses (W-BEAMS).** BM1 a long edge + piece edge (6 pieces) -> 6 span regions, each = the piece; BM2 CONTROL pieces are NOT bridged over the column gaps; BM3 vertical pair (end beam) found; BM4 CONTROL a parallel pair wider than the default is not a band; BM5 elevation band (top line + soffit pieces) gives z 122..132 in to the plan pieces by interval/centre; BM6 `@EXCLUDE` over the 8.7 in joint pair removes exactly those regions and counts them; BM7 CONTROL without the exclusion the joint IS a beam region (the module does not guess); BM8 `labelsFor` ignores titles; BM9 a label `B-1` within 0.8 m names its region, two different labels within range name nothing; BM10 `@BODYZ=beam,...` for regions without elevation evidence; browser (W-DXF-STRUCT S11): Layers offers `beam`, the exclusion row, Convert builds the spans; Real shed (W-DXF-SHED flow D, SKIP without sheets): floor + beam-layout + foundation plans + South/West/East: the beam spans of the long beams (A and D sides) and end beams as drawn, the joint excluded, parity counts vs `shed_scene.js` beams (oracle only), the B-1/B-2 depth conflict listed as advisory.
**Not in this slice:** bridging beams over columns into one continuous member; grade beams below grade (foundation-plan lines on `Dim`: same band rule, heights `@BODYZ`).

#### L9 Results (2026-10-18)
Built: `dxf2bodies.js` band regions (`bandRegions`; kinds beam / gradebeam / member; plan both orientations, elevation horizontal; a long edge + the pieces of the other edge -> one span per piece, NO merging of touching collinear pieces (a column's top line touches the soffit pieces and would fuse them: BM17), quantised sort (CAD float noise 1e-11 reordered pieces and the merge swallowed the shed's whole left block of beams: BM13; the same fix protects the column face pairs), `@EXCLUDE` honoured on the plan sheet (counted in `report.kinds.<kind>.excluded`), per-region type mark `B-1`/`B-2` (nearest unambiguous mark within 5 m), a span drawn in pieces lies WITHIN its bay's band and inherits it (only when the band is <= 3.5x the piece), sheet titles never name a kind, ONE elevation layer typed with a FRAME kind (column/beam/gradebeam/member) serves every frame kind but never a footing/plate (a column-typed elevation gave footings a height by centre line: caught by B24/B27)); editor: PER-SHEET layer typing (a shared layer name such as `Dim` is typed beam on ONE sheet: expander row per sheet, pooled row reads "(per sheet)", `setLayerRule(role, layer, ui, sheet)`); CLI: `--layer role:layer=kind@sheet`, `--exclude sheet:x0,y0,x1,y1`, `report` in verdict.json. **W-BEAMS BM1-BM17 PASS** (spans = pieces, not bridged, vertical end beams, width limit, elevation band z, `@EXCLUDE` removes exactly the joint and counts it, the joint IS a region without the exclusion, titles do not name, marks name B-1/B-2, ambiguous marks name nothing, `@BODYZ`, falsifier: column layer gives no bands, shed case: one column-typed elevation layer, float noise, within-rule, ambiguous band, size limit, column tops). W-DXF-STRUCT S11 (17/17): per-sheet typing + own-origin beam sheet. **Real shed:** CLI (W-CLI C13b-d) beam-layout plan typed beam on that sheet only: 17 spans at z 122..132 in from the South elevation band (22 wait: right block drawn as one continuous band, the end beams, the expansion joint); the user excludes the joint (4 regions: its 2 long pieces + 2 joint fragments) and states `beam=3.098794,3.352794`: **35 beam spans (17 drawn + 18 stated), 0 left out**: D row 15 spans, A row 14, 3 spans at each end; PARITY with the hand-built oracle: each long-beam row runs from the oracle's beam start to its end (sheet 3 -21508.23..-19960.23 = floor frame -26802.1..-25254.1) to 0.6 in. Page (W-DXF-SHED D1, 12/12): the same through the Layers per-sheet row: 17 beams, the other 22 + 32 footings wait in "Body heights". The drawing conflict surfaced, not reconciled: the South elevation draws BOTH blocks 10 in deep (z 122..132) while the label says `B-2 12"x12"` for the right block (the stated range is the drawn 10 in; a person who knows better states another).
**Not yet:** the Exclude tool drag is pre-existing UI (witnessed in W-DXF-META); a page witness of excluding the joint is not written here (the module + CLI are witnessed). Next: Q3 gable-end walls.

## L10 Pair every length with a number (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code; user: "pair every length with a number")
**Why (measured on the real shed source).** 266 linear DIMENSIONs; 197 are auto-measured (text = geometry by construction), 69 carry a typed override; of the 40 override texts that parse as feet-inch lengths 10 agree with the measured length and 30 do not (`410'-0"` x5 vs 1,560 in = 130 ft, ratio 0.317; `8'` vs 94.6 in; `16'-0"` vs 202.6 in; detail callouts `12"` vs 50-54 in = detail not to scale). Today a body's size comes from the scaled line and the stated number is only compared in the gate (`dim_text_mismatch`). A text is a person's claim, a line is geometry; nothing forces them to agree.
**Rule.**
1. **Length** = any drawn extent the product uses: a body edge or span (column face pair, beam span, footing side, wall run, grid bay), measured in the sheet's units from entity coordinates.
2. **Number** = a stated value for that extent, from (in order): a DIMENSION whose two definition points coincide with the length's end points (tol = `bodies.pair_max_width`-style yellow default, 0.5 in) or lie on its extension lines; a dimension CHAIN (several collinear DIMENSIONs summing to the length); a label on the length (`12"x12"`, `B-1 12"x10"`); a user-stated marking (`@BODYZ`, `@GRIDH`).
3. **Pairing** `{length_id, handle(s), measured, number|null, source, ratio}` is computed for every length; classes: `PAIRED_AGREE` (|measured-number| <= max(0.6 in, 1%)), `PAIRED_ROUNDED` (<= 6%), `PAIRED_CONFLICT` (> 6%, ratio reported), `UNPAIRED` (no number anywhere: the value is the measured length and is a yellow default), `NTS_DETAIL` (sheet or view flagged not-to-scale, e.g. a detail whose callouts conflict at one common ratio).
4. **The number is the value (user, 2026-10-18).** The paired number is what the model uses and what the user checks and corrects; the drawn line is evidence for it, never the authority. Every pair is an editable row (length id, number, source, drawn measure, ratio); a conflict is shown with both values and the stated number is used until the user changes it. An UNPAIRED length takes its measured value as a yellow default number, editable the same way. No silent choice between two numbers: the row says which one is in force and why.
4c. **Precedence between two numbers for the same length (drafting convention: written over scaled; larger scale / more specific sheet over smaller).** When two stated numbers pair with one length, the DEFAULT winner is, in order: (1) a user-stated marking (`@BY=user`); (2) the number on the sheet with the larger drawing scale (a detail over a plan; scale from the sheet's `$DIMSCALE`/viewport/title text, else unknown); (3) a number on a dimension chain/label over an override that conflicts with its own measure; (4) otherwise none wins and the row stays open. The default is a yellow row naming both numbers, their sheets and the rule applied; the user confirms or picks the other (`@BY=user`). Sheets or views declared NTS never win over a scaled one and are not checked against geometry. Both numbers stay in the report; nothing is dropped. Witnesses: P13 detail (larger scale) vs plan number: detail is in force, plan shown; P14 `@BY=user` beats both; P15 equal scale, equal rank: row open, no winner; P16 NTS view loses and raises no geometry conflict.
4a. **Best-effort alignment of sides.** A body has two or more sides; the number fixes the extent between them. Align by anchoring the side(s) the drawing and the grid pin down (grid line, datum, column centre, the side shared with a neighbour) and moving the free side by the number. If both sides are pinned and disagree with the number, keep the anchors, report `ALIGN:overconstrained` with the residual (drawn - number) and do not distort neighbours. Residuals are logged, not hidden.
4b. **Recalculate.** Editing a number re-runs only what depends on it (this body's extent, bodies sharing its anchored side, quantities/cost, IFC properties); the result is deterministic from {drawing, numbers}, so the same numbers always give the same model. Pairing + numbers are stored in the marked DXF (`DXF2BIM-MARK`, `@BY=user` once edited, `@BY=model` when derived) with no sidecar.
5. **Report:** `pairing.json` per sheet + summary counts; log `§PAIR sheet= lengths= agree= rounded= conflict= unpaired= nts=`.
**Witnesses (W-PAIR), each names the issue it proves.** P1 a dimension on a column face pair pairs with it (issue: size had no stated source); P2 CONTROL a dimension 1 in off the end points does not pair; P3 chain of 3 collinear dims pairs the whole bay; P4 override `12"` on a 50 in line = PAIRED_CONFLICT with ratio 4.17; P5 `410'-0"` vs 1,560 in = CONFLICT 0.317, exactly 1 yellow row for the 5 repeats; P6 auto-measured dim = PAIRED_AGREE; P7 a length with no dim/label = UNPAIRED + yellow default number; P10 editing a number resizes exactly that body and its dependants and nothing else (issue: line was the authority); P11 two pinned sides disagreeing with the number = `ALIGN:overconstrained` with residual, anchors unmoved; P12 same drawing + same numbers twice = identical bodies (determinism); P8 FALSIFIER: editing a number in the DXF text flips the class; P9 real shed (SKIP without sheets): per-sheet counts printed and every column/beam/footing length is in the report (0 silently missing).
**Not in this slice:** deciding which of two conflicting numbers is right (the user does); scale detection beyond the common-ratio NTS flag.

#### L10 Results (2026-10-18, slice 1: pure module `modeller/dxf2pair.js` + `tests/witness_dxf_pair.js`)
Built: number grammar (feet-inch, fractions, MTEXT codes stripped; `L/5`, `ld`, `Splice*` -> null, never guessed), `dimsOf`, `pair` (direct dimension on the end points, collinear CHAIN sum, label, user; classes AGREE / ROUNDED / CONFLICT / UNPAIRED / NTS_DETAIL / OPEN; precedence user > larger scale > agrees-with-own-measure > open; NTS numbers count only when nothing scaled states the length; repeats collapse to one yellow row), `align` (pinned sides stay, `ALIGN:overconstrained` + residual, `ALIGN:floating`). W-PAIR N1-N3, P1-P16 PASS (P2/P8/P15 are controls/falsifiers).
**Real shed, measured (P9/P9b):** the 160 dimensions in the plan/foundation/beam/truss sheets state PITCHES (centre to centre: 120, 200, 600 in), not member sizes. Pairing the 84 consecutive column/footing centre pitches: 84 agree, 0 conflict (all are auto-measured dims: agreement is by construction, the check that bites is the typed overrides, e.g. `410'-0"`). The 548 closed-rectangle sides on Col/Fo/Slab/Wall layers (12 in columns, 48x60 in footings) are ALL UNPAIRED by dimensions: their sizes are stated only by labels (`12"x12" R.C.C Column`) and the footing schedule on the details sheet. NEXT slice: the label/schedule source (parse `WxD`/`WxDxT` labels and schedule rows into the pair candidates, `@BY=file`), the sheet-scale source (title text / viewport; the split shed sheets carry no per-sheet scale so rule 4c step 2 does not fire there), editor rows + `@BY=user` persistence, then wiring into `dxf2bodies` (number in force drives the extent) with recalculation.
**L10 slice 2 (2026-10-18): labels as a number source.** `legendOf` reads `MARK = AxB` legend rows (`C-1 = 12"x12"`, `F-1 = 4'-0"x5'-0"`, `B-1 = 12" x 10"`; free text and `Column-12"x12"` are not legends), `markPoints` the instance marks, `labelRects` gives each rectangle the unambiguous nearest mark OF ITS KIND (prefix convention C=column F=footing B=beam GB=gradebeam, a named yellow default, overridable: the foundation plan puts C-1 and F-1 side by side, 38 vs 45 in from a footing, so distance alone is ambiguous) within half the same-kind pitch, and the legend's two numbers label the sides by best fit to the drawing (the legend does not say which number is which side). Controls: no mark in range -> `GAP:label_no_mark_in_range`, two kinds tied -> `GAP:label_ambiguous_mark`, nothing guessed. W-PAIR L1-L10 PASS. **Real shed (P9c):** foundation plan 64/64 rectangles labelled (32 columns 12x12, 32 footings 48x60 = the legend), 256 sides PAIRED_AGREE, 0 conflicts; the floor plan has no instance marks so its 32 columns stay UNPAIRED (128 sides, 128 yellow rows) until the sets are cross-matched by grid position (next). The legend is a set-level fact (the plan sheet states none, the foundation sheet does). Still to do: sheet scale source, details-sheet schedule rows, editor rows + `@BY=user`, wiring into `dxf2bodies`.
**L10 slice 3 (2026-10-18): cross-sheet inheritance.** The same members are drawn on several sheets at different origins; only one sheet carries the instance marks. `matchByTranslation` votes over all same-kind centre differences, keeps the winning translation only if it explains at least half of the smaller set (tolerance 1 sheet unit, a yellow default), then pairs one-to-one; `inheritLabels` gives each matched member the mark of its counterpart (the labelled lengths carry `via` = the source id). Controls: unmatched member -> `GAP:label_no_match`; unrelated layouts -> `GAP:label_sheets_not_alignable`, nothing inherited; kinds never cross; a matched member of the wrong size is a CONFLICT with the inherited label in force. W-PAIR X1-X5 PASS. **Real shed (P9c):** the floor plan's 32 columns map onto the foundation plan's 32 by translation (7370.3, 21.8) (32/32 matched, 0 unmatched); with the foundation plan's 64 rectangles that is 384 of 384 sides PAIRED_AGREE against the legend (12x12 columns, 48x60 footings), 0 conflicts, 0 unpaired.
**L10.6 (2026-10-18): the number drives the body (slice 4).** `dxf2bodies.js` now pairs every plan region of a non-band kind (column, footing, plate) with its stated size: set-level legend (`MARK = AxB`), instance marks (kind-aware), other plan sheets by translation vote, plus the sheet's dimensions; the number in force replaces the drawn extent on that axis, centred on the drawn centre (`ALIGN:floating`; no grid anchor is known yet), while the ELEVATION match still uses the DRAWN footprint (so a resized body keeps its height). `opts.numbers[guid] = {x, y}` (metres) is the user's number and beats every label (`source: user`); calling `buildBodies` again with the edited numbers is the recalculation (deterministic: same drawing + numbers = same bodies). Each body carries `size: {x,y: {drawn, number, class, source, rule, align}}`; `report.size_conflicts` lists every side whose stated number replaced the drawn extent (the yellow rows). Band kinds (beam, gradebeam, member) are NOT sized from the legend: `B-1 = 12" x 10"` is a CROSS-SECTION (width x depth), not the plan length; applying it to a 120 in span shortened the shed's beams to 12 in (caught by W-CLI C13d), so band legends are left for the cross-section slice (width -> plan width, depth -> z extent, with the elevation band as the cross-check). Witnesses: W-BODY-SIZE S0-S8 (control without legend, 0.30 -> 0.35 by label, why-record, volume = number, elevation match on the drawn footprint, user number beats label for that body only, determinism, far mark applies nothing, agreeing label), W-CLI C15 on the real shed: all 64 columns and footings carry their stated size and all 64 agree with the drawing (0 conflicts, extents unchanged). Browser loader and `sw.js` list `dxf2pair.js`.
**L10.7 (2026-10-18): band kinds take the legend as a CROSS-SECTION.** `B-1 = 12" x 10"` pairs with the plan WIDTH (extent across the span, centred on the drawn centre line) and the z DEPTH (soffit z0 kept, the top moves: a beam bears on its supports from below; a yellow default). The legend gives no order: the assignment that fits the drawing best is taken, a tie falls back to the legend order (width first). Span lengths never change. `opts.numbers[guid] = {w, d}` (metres) is the user's number. W-BODY-SIZE BS1-BS7; W-CLI C16 on the real shed: of the 35 beam spans, **13 (B-1, left block) agree with 12" x 10" in width and depth; 17 (B-2, right block) agree in width but the elevation draws them 10 in deep against B-2 = 12": a visible PAIRED_CONFLICT with the label number in force (the long-known drawing conflict, now a row the user resolves, not something the code reconciles); 5 end beams have no mark nearby and stay as drawn.** A tie-break epsilon (1e-9) fixes float noise in the best-fit assignment (also in `labelRects`).
**L10.8 (2026-10-18): the dimension audit.** `auditDims` checks every TYPED dimension override against the dimension's own measured length, per sheet unit: auto-measured dims are only counted (they agree by construction); an override is AGREE / ROUNDED / CONFLICT (ratio = measured / stated) / NOT_A_LENGTH (`L/5`, `ld`, notes: never guessed) / NTS_DETAIL (declared not-to-scale sheets, listed, never a conflict); repeats collapse to one row per (text, measure). `dxf2bim_cli.js` writes it to `verdict.json` (`dimension_audit`) and prints `§DIMAUDIT`. **Real shed, all 160 dimensions of the plan, foundation, beam and truss sheets: 133 auto-measured, 2 agree, 4 rounded, 15 CONFLICT (16 rows), 6 not a length**; the largest is `410'-0"` x5 vs 1,560 in (ratio 0.3171). W-PAIR D1-D5, W-CLI C17. NTS is passed by the caller (`opts.nts`): a marking for it needs a BIMCompiler grammar change (next); the schedule-rows source is not needed on the shed (the legend already states C-1, F-1, B-1, B-2).

## L11 HATCH boundary loops (spec 2026-10-18; first slice built; RESUME queue Q3 and the parser gaps)
**Evidence (shed source, measured).** 143 HATCH entities (164 boundary loops) in the 11 sheets: `Wall/BRICK_STRBOND` 40 (the long side walls on the North/South faces), `Wall/BLOCKS` 16 (the gable-end brick: West and East, some entities hold several loops), `Wall/ANSI31` 10 and `0/ANSI31` 8 (cut fills in the sections), `Dim/ANSI31` 38 (arrow heads, small fills), `Dim/ZIGZAG` 10 (the East-end zigzag above the low middle bay), `Dim/CONCRETE1` 7, `Dim/AR-CONC` 4, `Dim/DOTS2` 2, `Win/ANSI31` 8. `dxf-parser` has no HATCH: until now none of this reached the reader, so the gable walls and side walls were only ever built by a hand script.
**Rule (slice 1, `modeller/dxf2hatch.js`, pure).** `parseHatches(text)` reads HATCH entities from the ASCII DXF text: layer, pattern, solid flag, and EVERY boundary loop in order (polyline paths with bulge arcs flattened at 10 degrees; edge paths of LINE and ARC edges chained end to end whatever each edge's direction). Nothing is dropped silently: edges that do not close within the tolerance -> `GAP:hatch_loop_open` (the loop is kept, `closed=false`, never passed off as a region); an ELLIPSE/SPLINE edge -> `GAP:hatch_edge_unsupported`, no loop invented. One entity with several loops gives several loops of ONE hatch (the importer must give each body its own id, `-L<i>`).
**Slice 2 (next, own results section when built).** The hatch layer is typed by the user like any other layer (`@LAYER=Wall,<kind>` with a hatch-aware kind); an ELEVATION hatch loop is a face region (x along the face axis, z through the aligned map) and the body is that polygon extruded by a thickness: thickness from the label (`10" Brick Wall` with its leader tip inside the panel) as a yellow default, else the plan wall pair; openings (the fan holes) are the gaps in the outline. The pattern name is evidence of kind (BRICK_STRBOND / BLOCKS = masonry; ANSI31 on a section = cut; ZIGZAG = owner question, built as nothing); it is shown, never decided.
**Witness W-HATCH H1-H9.** H1 polyline path; H2 edge path with mixed edge directions chained; H3 one entity with 3 loops; H4 arc edge flattened (area = box + half disc); H5 CONTROL open loop = gap; H6 CONTROL ellipse = gap, no loop; H7 bulge adds/removes exactly the half-disc area by sign; H8 FALSIFIER no hatches -> none; H9 real shed: 143 raw = 143 parsed, 164 loops, 0 open, 0 gaps.
**L10.9 (2026-10-18): the user's number, persisted and editable.** Grammar (BIMCompiler #213, master 27839c82a, synced): `@SIZE=<body id>,<x|y|w|d>,<metres>` (x,y = plan extents of a column/footing/plate; w,d = width,depth of a band kind), `@BY=user`, conflicting restatement = `MARK:conflict`, unknown axis / non-positive = `MARK:malformed` (BIMCompiler LM10). `buildBodies` reads the sheets' `desc.sizes`, then `opts.numbers` (the caller's edits win over the file). Editor: a "Stated sizes" section lists every side where the stated number replaced the drawn extent (drawn -> stated, source) with an input prefilled with the stated number; typing another and Set writes the marking on the plan sheet and the body is recalculated; the written marking is listed with a remove button, which restores the label's number. CLI: `--size <body id>:<x|y|w|d>=<metres>`. Witnesses: W-DXF-SIZES Z1-Z5 (page: 4 differing sides listed, Set 0.4 resizes exactly that body with source user, marking in the effective sheet text, survives re-open, remove restores 0.35, bad input writes nothing), W-CLI C18-C19, W-DXF-STRUCT 17/17 and W-DXF-ATTN 17/17 unchanged.
**L11 slice 2 (2026-10-18): wall panels from elevation hatch loops (RESUME queue Q3).** Built: `modeller/dxf2panels.js` (pure) + `dxf2hatch.js parseLeaders/inPoly` + the importer reads hatches into every sheet that has them (`sheet.hatches`, `sheet.hatchLayers`) + BODY kind `panel` (BIMCompiler #214 grammar, IFC `IfcWall/STANDARD`) + `dxf2structure.judge` builds panels beside the other bodies + the editor lists hatch-only layers and offers `panel`; CLI `--layer elev:Wall=panel` accepts hatch-only layers.
**Rule.** The user types the elevation's hatch layer `panel` (`@LAYER=Wall,panel`). Every CLOSED loop of those hatches is a face polygon (along the face through the aligned map, z through `@Z`); the body is that polygon extruded by a thickness, centred on the face plane, mesh = the existing ear-clipped prism with the axes permuted onto the face (even permutation for minX/maxX, odd + winding flip for minY/maxY; volume sign checked). **Thickness, in order:** the user's `@SIZE=<panel id>,w,<m>` (panel ids `body-panel-<sheet#>-<handle>[-L<i>]`) > a thickness label (`10" Brick Wall`) whose LEADER TIP lies inside the panel (each leader belongs to the nearest thickness label within 1 m of its far end; on the shed the far end is 21 in from the text start) > the label of a panel of the same sheet that overlaps it in height by >= 50% (provenance derived, yellow default `panel.thickness_inherited`) > `GAP:panel_no_thickness` and no body (the hint names the `@SIZE` to state). Two labels pointing into one panel = `GAP:panel_label_ambiguous`; two different labels reaching the same height band = `GAP:panel_thickness_ambiguous`. **Plane:** the outermost grid line on the face's side of the base plan, centred (yellow default `panel.plane`: the elevation does not say flush or centred). A loop inside another loop of its hatch is a HOLE: `GAP:panel_hole_unsupported`, the outer panel is still built, the hole is reported not hidden. Open loops are `GAP:hatch_loop_open` (L11 slice 1).
**Also fixed on the way (found on the real West elevation).** `PR-Z-GROUND` required the ground line group to cover 80% of the SHEET width; the shed's West ground line is 764 of a 962 in wide sheet (79%: leaders and a title block widen it) so the 950 in beam-top line above it was proposed as z = 0. Now: the lowest line group that is at least 60% of the LONGEST group (and 30% of the sheet). W-SUGGEST SG5b.
**Witnesses.** W-PANELS PN1-PN15 (thickness from the leader label, 0.254 m, centred on x = 1.0 m; volume = area x thickness; triangular gable 0.0635 m3; inherited thickness; no label = gap and no body; user number; no overlap = no inheritance; untyped layer = nothing; hole = gap; two loops = two ids; minY face; determinism; structure judge builds them), W-DXF-PANELS P1-P5 (page: hatch-only layer listed and offers `panel`, structure set 254/127 mm, real Convert = 2 IfcWall inserts, round trip), W-CLI C20-C21 on the real shed.
**Real shed (West + East + plan, typed `Wall` = panel, the two PL datums stated, nothing else): 12 panels = the hand-built oracle's 12 bodies**: West 3 lower panels 10 in (z 24..120 in) + 3 gable tops 5 in (z 132..192.7/224.6 in), East 3 lower panels 5 in (the middle bay 24..60 in) + 3 gable tops 5 in; 2 labelled directly, 10 inherited (the drawing labels one lower and one gable panel per end), 0 left out; every mesh volume = drawn hatch loop area x thickness (worst relative error 1.6e-7: vertex rounding). Not done: the side walls on the North/South faces (`BRICK_STRBOND`, 40 hatches) are the same machinery but their thickness label is `5" Support Wall, H-24"` (not a "Brick Wall" label) and the hatches reach 98 in against the 24 in in the text (an owner question, recorded, not reconciled); the fan openings are the gaps of the outline; the East ZIGZAG hatch is built as nothing (owner question).

## L12 Entity accounting (2026-10-18; built with its first slice)
**Issue.** Entities the reader cannot parse vanish without a trace (dxf-parser has no HATCH or LEADER; INSERT block expansion is not built). Every model-space entity must be READ by the product or LISTED as dropped. `modeller/dxf2account.js` (pure): `account(text, parsedEntities, own)` counts the ENTITIES-section types of the raw text (block definitions are not model space) against what dxf-parser returned and what our own modules read (HATCH by `dxf2hatch.parseHatches`, LEADER by `parseLeaders`); `dropped = raw - parsed - own`, listed per type with its count; the CLI prints `§ACCOUNT` and writes `verdict.json entity_account`. W-ACCOUNT A1-A6 (counts; dropped listed; block contents are not model space; own modules named; FALSIFIER without them the same hatch/leader are dropped).
**Real shed, measured (11 sheets, 3,531 model-space entities): nothing is dropped** once HATCH (143) and LEADER (109) are read by our own modules: LINE 1,906, LWPOLYLINE 436, TEXT 408, CIRCLE 187, DIMENSION 196, ARC 77, MTEXT 59, SOLID 10 are parsed by dxf-parser. The earlier worry list was wrong for these sheets: the 646 INSERTs, 1,282 POINTs and most MTEXT of the SOURCE file sit inside BLOCK definitions (dimension decoration `_DotSmall`/`DIM1`/`AR`, anonymous `*D…`/`*X…` blocks) or were left outside the frames by the sheet splitter (the source model space has 42 INSERTs; the split sheets have none). **INSERT / block expansion is therefore a capability gap for OTHER sets (a BricsCAD file with window/door blocks), not for this shed**: it stays in the queue until a set needs it, and the accounting will list it by count when it appears (`dropped=INSERT:n`). C22 (W-CLI) asserts the shed's account is empty.

## L13 A coherent building at best effort: synthetic completion, material dress, a bill with stated formulas (spec 2026-10-18, BEFORE code)
**Direction (user, 2026-10-18).** The aim is a coherent, finished building at best effort. If the user supplies every fact (the green ones), the model is exact; if not, it approximates, and the user, who knows the building, corrects it. Synthetic parts are shown tinted or material-dressed so they are recognisable, but their material CONTENT is sharp: a wall, a roof, a floor slab, the ground works have a roughly known cost, with the formulas stated in a JSON reference.
**Evidence tint.** `prov` on every body: `drawn` (no tint), `derived` (computed from drawn evidence plus a convention: amber `0xffb347`), `assumed` (no drawing evidence for existence or position: red `0xff2d2d`, as today). The tint is a display colour only; the material is separate.
**L13.1 The reference and the bill (first slice).**
- `modeller/synthetic_ref.json` (versioned, public, data only): per IFC class a **part entry** `{material, quantity: {basis, formula, unit}, factor?, notes}`. Formulas are tiny arithmetic strings over named MEASURES of the body's mesh: `V` volume, `A` the area of the largest pair of parallel faces (the plate/wall/slab face; computed by area-weighted normal buckets, exact for prisms), `t = V / A` thickness, `L` longest bounding-box dimension, `N` count. The evaluator accepts numbers, `+ - * /`, parentheses and these names only (no functions, no code).
- **Rates are never invented here.** `rate_ref.class` points at a rate-pack class (`viewer/rates/<pack>.json` `materials[class]`: `rate`, `unit`, `desc`); a class with no pack rate costs 0 and is listed (`no_rate`), exactly as the Find cost does. The entry's formula basis must match the pack unit (M2 area, M3 volume, M length, EA count) or the line is flagged `unit_mismatch`. A thickness factor (`t / t_ref`, t_ref read from the pack description, e.g. `Blockwork Wall 150mm` -> 0.15 m) scales a per-area rate to the actual thickness.
- `modeller/dxf2bill.js` (pure): `bill(bodies, ref, pack) -> { lines: [{id, kind, class, material, prov, measures, quantity, unit, rate, pack_class, pack_desc, factor, amount, formula, flags}], totals: {amount, by_prov: {drawn, derived, assumed}, by_class}, flags }`. Deterministic; every line shows its formula, its inputs and where the rate came from; the totals split drawn / derived / assumed so the user sees how much of the cost rests on assumptions. CLI: `--pack <name>` (default `cidb2024_my`) writes `bill.json` and prints `§BILL`.
**L13.2 Synthetic completion (next slice).** Ground slab over the grid footprint (thickness = the pack's slab description, 250 mm), gabled roof from the end elevation's gable profile (eaves at the gable base corners, ridge at the apex, length = the grid extent; sheet thickness a yellow default; overhang default 0, the drawing's 18 in is an owner question), each `assumed` or `derived` with the tint above and its formula in the bill. Trusses, doors, fans and EC pads follow the same pattern when their owner facts are stated, else as assumed bodies with yellow defaults.
**L13.3 IFC dress (after).** IfcMaterial + IfcRelAssociatesMaterial from the entry's material in both IFC writers, and the tint as a surface style.
**Witnesses (W-BILL).** BL1 formula evaluator: arithmetic and names only; a function call, an unknown name, an assignment or a string is refused (no code execution); BL2 measures of a known prism: V, A, t, L exact; BL3 a 3 x 2 x 0.15 m wall slab: quantity A = 6 m2, rate and unit from the pack class, amount = rate x 6 x (0.15/0.15); the same wall 0.30 m thick costs twice per area (factor 2); BL4 CONTROL a class with no pack rate: amount 0, flag no_rate, not priced at a guess; BL5 CONTROL unit mismatch flagged; BL6 totals split by provenance and equal the sum of lines; BL7 deterministic; BL8 real shed (SKIP without sheets): every body of the structure chain has a line, the formulas and pack classes shown, totals by provenance.
**L13.1 results (2026-10-18).** Built: `modeller/synthetic_ref.json` (10 part entries: IfcWall, IfcSlab, IfcRoof, IfcFooting, IfcColumn, IfcBeam, IfcMember, IfcPlate, IfcDoor, IfcWindow; material, quantity formula over V/A/t/L/N, thickness factor `t / t_ref` for the per-area classes, materialByLabel for the wall), `modeller/dxf2bill.js` (arithmetic-only evaluator: a function call, statement, assignment, string, property access, unknown name, division by zero or dangling operator is refused; mesh measures with A = the largest pair of parallel faces; deterministic), CLI `--pack` + `bill.json` + `§BILL`. W-BILL BL1-BL10 PASS (including the hostile-formula refusal, the falsifier that editing the JSON formula changes the amount, and that every part entry has a pack class and the pack's unit).
**One product run over six real sheets (plan, South, beam layout, foundation, West, East; layers typed, the two PL datums and the footing/beam ranges stated): 111 bodies, 0 left out** = 32 IfcColumn + 35 IfcBeam + 32 IfcFooting + 12 IfcWall gable panels; **bill RM 154,837 with the pack `cidb2024_my`: drawn 104,074 / derived 50,763 (the panels whose thickness was inherited, and the beams whose range the user stated) / assumed 0**; columns 32 x 1.89 m, beams 98.6 m, footings 18.1 m3, panels 69.9 m2 (thickness factor 1.69 for the 10 in panels against the pack's 150 mm). W-CLI C23-C24. Caveats stated, not hidden: the rates are the pack's class rates (e.g. IfcColumn is "Structural Steel Column" per metre while the shed's columns are RCC: the line shows `pack_desc`); the footing rate is the pack's M3 conversion of its EA rate (viewer/rates.js `volFactor`), so footing cost is the pack's own and low. Multi-elevation caveat found: typing the shared layer `0` as `column` on the West and East elevations as well makes their end-face regions disagree with the South heights (30 columns left out); type the layer on the elevation that shows the columns (`elev:0=column@1S_ELEV`).
**L13.2 results (2026-10-18): best-effort completion.** Built: `modeller/dxf2synth.js` (pure) + `dxf2structure.judge` opt-in `ctx.complete` + CLI `--complete` + the importer tints `derived` bodies AMBER (`0xffb347`; `assumed` stays red `0xff2d2d`, drawn untinted) + KIND `slab` (IfcSlab/BASESLAB). **Ground slab:** footprint = the outermost grid lines of the base plan, top = the lowest base of the drawn columns (they stand on it; no drawn column = `GAP:synth_slab_no_columns`, no slab), thickness = the rate pack's slab description (`RC Slab 250mm` -> 0.25 m); defaults `synth.slab.thickness`, `synth.slab.footprint`. **Gabled roof:** eaves at the base of the end-gable panels (the highest panel base on the plane of the panel that reaches the ridge), ridge at the apex, length = the distance between the two gable planes (one end only = `GAP:synth_roof_one_end`, no gable = `GAP:synth_roof_no_gable`), sheet thickness a display default (`synth.roof.sheet_thickness` 0.0254 m; cost is by area), overhang 0 until stated (`synth.roof.overhang`; the drawing's 18 in is an owner question). All `derived` (computed from drawn evidence plus a stated convention), so the bill's drawn / derived / assumed split shows how much rests on them. W-SYNTH SY0-SY10 (slab footprint/level/volume 6 m3, roof volume = length x slope length x t, slope = atan(0.5/2), overhang option, the three gap controls, determinism, the bill prices them), W-CLI C25-C26.
**Real shed with `--complete` (six real sheets): 114 bodies = 111 drawn + 1 slab (604 m2, top 24 in, 0.25 m) + 2 roof sheets (642 m2 total, eave 132 in, ridge 224.6 in, slope about 17 deg); bill RM 479,638 = drawn 104,074 + derived 375,564 + assumed 0** (slab 172,103; roof 152,698 at the pack's `IfcSlab` 285/m2 and `IfcRoof` 238/m2). The roof slope from the gables (about 17.2 deg) agrees with the truss section's 17.5 deg label; the difference is not reconciled. Trusses, doors, fans and EC pads remain: they follow the same pattern (assumed, red) when built.

## L14 The truss section repeated along the building (spec 2026-10-18; built, RESUME queue Q5)
**Direction (user, 2026-10-18).** Best effort: if the user supplies every green fact the model is exact, otherwise it approximates and is tinted so the user sees which parts are synthetic. A truss is drawn once, in a section; the building has one at many places.
**Rule.** `modeller/dxf2section.js` (pure). The user types the layers of a SECTION sheet (`@VIEW=SECTION` with `@HAXIS`, `@CUT=<X|Y>,<coord>`, `@GRIDH` >= 2, `@Z` >= 2; BIMCompiler kind `bar`): a **`bar` layer is the truss layer**: each LINE (>= 150 mm) is a member axis, each closed outline a gusset plate, each open outline a purlin run; a `plate` layer holds plates only, a `member` layer runs only. **Stations:** the cut is where the drawn section sits; copies repeat at the spacing (`section.spacing`, default = the smallest column pitch of the base plan along the cut axis, yellow; the user states another with `@SIZE=section-spacing,x,<m>`) over the grid extent, the cut included. **The copy at the cut is `derived` (drawn evidence), every other copy `assumed` (red)**; a purlin run is `derived` (its position is drawn, its length is the span between the first and last station). **Member size:** the user's `@SIZE=<bar id>,w` > the size label (`1.5"x1.5"x1/4" Angle`, `2 - 2"x2"x1/4"`: the first leg is the square envelope; a label that names another element, `Beam-1 (12"x10")`, is not a size) whose LEADER TIP touches the bar (each leader belongs to the nearest label within 1 m of its far end; the tip must lie within 100 mm of the bar axis) > the most common labelled size (yellow default `section.bar.size`) > `GAP:bar_no_size` and no body; two different labels at one bar = `GAP:bar_label_ambiguous`. **Plate thickness:** the user's `@SIZE=<plate id>,w` > the sheet's ONE `... Thickness Plate` label (a quote mark means inches whatever the sheet unit; yellow default because the label points at no plate) > `GAP:plate_no_thickness`. Meshes: a square-section box along the bar axis in the section plane (outward windings by volume sign), the ear-clipped prism for plates; ids `body-bar-<sheet#>-<handle>-S<k>`, `body-plate-...`, `body-run-...`. Suggestions added to the editor/CLI: `PR-VIEW` accepts the title `Section X-X (...)`, `PR-HAXIS` for sections, and **`PR-CUT`**: the two equal one-letter labels of the section line on the plan (exactly one aligned pair; two pairs = refused, listed).
**Witnesses.** W-SECTION SE1-SE17 (stations, label-by-leader sizes, geometry and volume, derived/assumed split, default and gap controls, ambiguity, a label naming another element is not a size, user size, user spacing, plates with a stated thickness, purlin run, determinism, the structure judge), W-SUGGEST SG10-SG12 (the title, PR-CUT, the two-pairs refusal), W-CLI C27-C28 on the real shed.
**Real shed (plan + South + the truss detail section, nothing else typed but the layer and the PL datum): 32 columns + 12 bars x 13 stations + 9 gussets x 13 + 12 purlin runs = 317 bodies, 0 left out** (the oracle's hand-built truss set was 318 bodies); cut x = -26256.4 in found from the plan's two `A` labels; copies every 120 in from -26736.4 to -25296.4 in; sizes 1.5 in angles and 2 in chords from the drawing's own labels, 7 unlabelled bars take the 1.5 in modal size (yellow), plate thickness 1/4 in from the label (yellow); bill RM 423,904: drawn 75,692 / derived 156,433 / **assumed 191,779** (the repeated trusses). Open owner facts, carried as yellow defaults and never decided: the true truss spacing (the drawing shows one section at no column line), whether the right block repeats the left, angle-leg orientation. The expansion joint (8.7 in between grids 06 and 35) is not modelled in the station set.
**L13.2 on the page (2026-10-18).** The import panel offers "Complete the building (floor slab, roof): amber, estimated" on a structure set; ticking re-judges with `complete`, adds the slab and the two roof sheets (derived, amber `0xffb347`) and the four `synth.*` yellow defaults; Convert puts real IfcSlab/IfcRoof inserts on the canvas; unticking removes them and the defaults. W-DXF-COMPLETE K1-K4 (page): 8 -> 11 bodies, 11 inserts incl. IfcSlab/IfcRoof, the last insert amber + derived, back to 8. Page loader and `sw.js` list `dxf2section.js`, `dxf2synth.js`.
**L13.3 results (2026-10-18): the material dress.** `Dxf2Bill.materialOf(class, label, ref)` gives each body the reference's part material (label overrides: `Brick masonry` for a "Brick Wall" panel); `dxf2structure.judge` writes it into `ifc.material`; both IFC writers (`dxf2ifc.js` headless, `bonsai_ifc.js` page) create one `IFCMATERIAL` per distinct name and one `IFCRELASSOCIATESMATERIAL` per material over its products (`§IFC-MATERIAL`). The page loads `synthetic_ref.json` with its scripts (`state.ref`); the CLI reads it from the repo. W-CLI C13m (3 columns -> IFCMATERIAL 'Reinforced concrete' associated to all 3), W-DXF-COMPLETE K3c (page IFC4: Reinforced concrete for columns and slab, Brick masonry for the panels, Profiled metal roofing for the roof, 3 associations). The tint stays a display colour on the canvas (derived amber, assumed red); the IFC material is separate and sharp.
**L11.2 addendum (2026-10-18): side walls and the label height.** The thickness label is now any one descriptor word + "wall" (`10" Brick Wall`, `5" Support Wall, H-24"`); a label that also states a height (`H-24"`) is compared with the drawn panel height by the number rule: more than 6% apart = advisory `GAP:panel_label_height` (the drawn panel is still built; PN17/PN18). **Real shed, North + South (typed `Wall` = panel, the two PL datums): 29 panels** (14 South + 15 North: 19 at 96/98 in and 10 low-bay panels of 36 in), all 5 in thick from the drawing's `5" Brick Wall` label (2 labelled directly, 27 inherited by height), 0 left out (W-CLI C29). The `5" Support Wall, H-24"` label is on the FLOOR PLAN, not on the elevations, so no height advisory fires on the shed: the owner question stands (the 24 in of the plan text against the 96/98 in brick drawn on the elevations). Not modelled: the openings in these walls (fans, doors: the `Win` layer), the East ZIGZAG hatch.
**L11.3 fans (2026-10-18).** A layer typed `fan` (BIMCompiler kind, #216) on an elevation: concentric CIRCLEs (centres within 1 mm) are ONE fan, the outer circle is the housing diameter, the hub is counted (`rings`); the body is a 24-sided disc of that diameter, `fan.depth` deep (yellow default 0.3 m; `@SIZE=<fan id>,w,<m>` states it), centred on the wall plane (the end grid line, as the panels), IfcFan/PROPELLORAXIAL, `derived` (amber), priced as the pack's `IfcFlowMovingDevice` (the pack has no fan class; `synthetic_ref.json` says so). Both IFC writers know `IFCFAN` and now write the PredefinedType of IfcSlab (BASESLAB) and IfcFan. W-PANELS PN19-PN23, W-CLI C30. **Real shed (West + East + plan): 9 IfcFan, 55 in (1.397 m) across, centred at z 92.5 in on the grid-01 plane, y from -4320 to -3800 in, beside the 12 gable panels = 21 bodies, bill RM 42,248.** The fans sit in the openings the panel outlines leave (the fan-hole gaps). Still to do: doors (the drawn gaps of the support walls, no height drawn), EC pads (plan outline, thickness in a detail section), the East ZIGZAG hatch (owner question).

## L15 The whole shed from the drawings, one product run (2026-10-18)
`node modeller/dxf2bim_cli.js --sheets <eight real sheets> --complete --ifc --accept-suggestions --set SHED --level 0 --datum ... --layer ...` (the exact line is `ALLARGS` in `tests/witness_dxf_cli.js` C31) reads the plan, South, North, West and East elevations, the beam layout, the foundation plan and the truss section **in 0.7 s** and writes the bodies, the bill and a typed IFC4 with materials. Nothing is hand-built; the only facts the person states are the layers (which CAD layer is a column, footing, beam, wall-panel hatch, fan, truss), the five PL datums (+24 in at each elevation's PL line), the footing depth, the beam range, and the expansion-joint exclusion.
**441 bodies, 0 left out: 32 IfcColumn, 35 IfcBeam, 32 IfcFooting, 41 IfcWall (12 gable-end + 29 side-wall panels), 13 IfcFan, 168 IfcMember (12 truss bars x 13 stations + 12 purlin runs), 117 IfcPlate (9 gussets x 13), 1 IfcSlab, 2 IfcRoof.** Entity account: 2,368 model-space entities, none dropped. IFC4 typed 441/441, read back by the usual extractor (elements = 441). Bill (pack `cidb2024_my`): **RM 887,975 = drawn 105,782 + derived 590,414 (slab, roof, panels with inherited thickness, stated beam ranges, fans, purlin runs, the trusses at the cut) + assumed 191,779 (the repeated trusses)**; every line names its formula, its measures and the pack class the rate came from. W-CLI C31-C33.
**What is still the owner's:** the truss spacing and whether the right block repeats the left (yellow `section.spacing`), the roof sheet thickness and overhang (`synth.roof.*`), the slab thickness and extent (`synth.slab.*`), the fan depth (`fan.depth`), the 24 in plan text against the 96/98 in brick drawn on the elevations, doors, EC pads, the East ZIGZAG hatch, the B-2 beam depth (10 in drawn, 12 in labelled), the `410'-0"` dimension text against the 1,560 in drawn. None is hidden: each is a yellow default or a visible conflict row.
**L11.4 nets (2026-10-18, owner: "Zigzag likely net as it's a farm").** The East-end ZIGZAG hatch (owner question) is a ventilation NET. A layer typed `net` (BIMCompiler kind, #217) on an elevation reads only the hatches whose pattern is a mesh pattern (ZIGZAG, MESH, NET, WIRE; an ANSI31 arrow head or a section fill on the same layer is not a net, PN25); every closed loop is a thin IfcCovering/CLADDING sheet over the opening, centred on the wall plane, thickness `net.thickness` (yellow default 5 mm, not drawn; `@SIZE=<net id>,w,<m>`), `derived` (amber), material Netting, priced by area as the pack's `IfcCovering` (a floor/ceiling finish per m2, the nearest class; `synthetic_ref.json` says so). The drawing stores each zigzag twice (two hatch objects with the same boundary): they are ONE net. W-PANELS PN24-PN27, W-CLI C34. **Real shed: 3 nets** (East 480 x 60 in over the low middle bay; South and North 600 x 60 in over the low bays), all from z 60 to 120 in (the low bays below them are the 36 in panels from 24 to 60 in). The whole-shed run (C31) is now **444 bodies** (+3 IfcCovering), IFC4 read back as 444. IfcCovering added to the IFC writers' class map and PredefinedType enum.

**L12 addendum: the well-formed sheets (2026-10-18).** The first frame split dropped the anonymous dimension geometry blocks, so every DIMENSION of the 11 shed sheets was invalid for a strict loader (the reader never noticed: dxf-parser is lenient). Local tool `ShedDrawing/tools/split_sheets.py` (ezdxf Importer: blocks, layers, linetypes, styles; copies each DIMENSION's `*D` block and re-points the reference) regenerates `sheets_v2/`: identical per-type entity counts to the old sheets for all 11, **0 ezdxf recover fixes, 0 errors, 196 of 196 dimensions valid**; the product gives the SAME numbers on both (W-CLI C10-C34 with `SHED_SHEETS=.../sheets_v2/`: 444 bodies, same bill). The marked sheets the product writes still lack a `DXF2BIM-MARK` layer-table entry (tolerated by every reader tried, `ezdxf` audit clean): a writer fix in BIMCompiler `dxf2marks.writeMarks` if a strict CAD loader ever needs it. Private backup: `private/dxf2bim/local_2026-10-18f.tgz` (bucket `bim-ootb-backup`, not public).

## L16 Doors from plan swing symbols (2026-10-18; owner: "go for default yellow")
**Evidence (shed plan, layer `Rod`).** Three `Door` labels sit at the drawing's doors. Each is drawn as the usual symbol: an ARC about the hinge (radius = the leaf width, a quarter circle) and the OPEN leaf as a line (here a pair of lines 1.25 in apart = the leaf thickness) from the hinge to one end of the arc. The closed leaf is the other end of the arc. West end near grid A and near grid D: 33 in leaves; east side near grid 42: a double leaf, 2 x 27 in. Four more swings of 21.7 in have NO drawn open leaf. No height is drawn anywhere, and the elevations show no door outline.
**Rule.** `modeller/dxf2doors.js` (pure; BIMCompiler kind `doorswing`, #218): on a plan layer typed `doorswing` every ARC sweeping 60-190 degrees is a door leaf; the line(s) from the hinge to a point one radius away identify the OPEN end of the arc, so the CLOSED leaf runs from the hinge the other way, one radius long; thickness = the spacing of two parallel open-leaf lines (drawn) else `door.thickness` (yellow default 0.04 m); height = `door.height` (yellow default 2.1 m; `@SIZE=<door id>,d,<m>` and `,w,<m>` state height and thickness); floor = the lowest drawn column base (else 0); IfcDoor/DOOR, `derived` (amber), priced as the pack's `IfcDoor` per leaf. A swing with no drawn open leaf is **not built** and listed (`GAP:door_closed_unknown`): which end of the arc is the closed leaf cannot be told. Not done: cutting the opening out of the wall panel the door stands in (the panels still run through the gap).
**Witnesses.** W-DOORS DR1-DR10 (closed leaf along +x from a 0..90 deg arc, drawn thickness from the double line, volume, defaults, single-line leaf default, no-leaf gap, circle and short arc ignored, right-hand hinge, user height/thickness, floor from columns, two doors + determinism), W-CLI C35. **Real shed: 4 IfcDoor** (two 33 in = 0.839 m end doors, the 2 x 27 in = 0.685 m double leaf), leaf thickness 1.25 in from the drawing, height 2.1 m (yellow); the 4 swings of 21.7 in are listed. Whole shed C31: **448 bodies** (+4 doors), IFC4 read back as 448.

These are not blockers. Each is either a yellow default the product applies and lists, a visible conflict row, or an item the drawing itself declares open. They are carried in the specs, never decided in code.
| item | state | where |
|---|---|---|
| truss spacing, right block = left block | default: the column pitch from the cut; copies assumed (red) | `section.spacing`, §L14 |
| roof sheet thickness, overhang | defaults 0.0254 m, 0 | `synth.roof.*`, §L13.2 |
| floor slab thickness, extent | defaults 0.25 m (the pack), outermost grid lines | `synth.slab.*`, §L13.2 |
| fan depth | default 0.3 m | `fan.depth`, §L11.3 |
| net thickness (the ZIGZAG hatches, owner: a net) | default 5 mm | `net.thickness`, §L11.4 |
| door height, single-line leaf thickness | door.height: DRAWN when the elevations' `Win` layer is typed `elevdoor` (96 / 94 in on the shed, §L25), else default 2.1 m; thickness default 0.04 m | `door.height`, `door.thickness`, §L16, §L25 |
| the four 21.7 in swings with no drawn open leaf | listed, not built: unlabelled, 75.3 degree sweep, hinge off the wall line; likely not doors (§L21.2) | `GAP:door_closed_unknown`, §L16 |
| EC pads | NOT built: the plan labels say "Length may vary", only one of the three carries a leader, no pad outline is drawn and the thickness sits in a detail section; nothing to extrude without inventing an extent | owner item |
| 24 in plan text vs 96/98 in brick on the elevations | both kept; the brick is built as drawn | §L11.2 |
