| `410'-0"` dimension text vs 1,560 in drawn | conflict row in the dimension audit | §L10.8 |
| doors cutting their opening in the wall panel | closed by measurement: no door overlaps any wall body, each stands in a gap bounded by walls (W-FULLCIRCLE F8); the cut is built only if a drawing's panel runs through a door | §L21.2 |
| steel/RCC class mismatch in the rate pack (IfcColumn priced as steel), no netting/fan rate | rates are the pack's class rates; the line shows the pack description | §L13.1 |

## L18 Element defaults: settings ready for every element type (2026-10-18; owner direction)
**Direction.** A 2D layout carries a measure only when it comes from a fully formed IFC model (meshed, with its properties). Everything else is a SETTING: a yellow-band field that holds a defaulted standard value or a guess, so the person knows they need to give a value; if they do not, the default determines the measurement. Defaults must be ready for (nearly) every element type, not only the ones a builder exists for today.
**Registry.** `modeller/element_defaults.json` (data only): 40 dotted keys across 20 IFC classes (IfcWall, IfcSlab, IfcRoof, IfcDoor, IfcWindow, IfcColumn, IfcBeam, IfcFooting, IfcPile, IfcStair, IfcRailing, IfcRamp, IfcCovering, IfcCurtainWall, IfcPlate, IfcMember, IfcFan and the synthesis settings), each `{element, unit, kind, value, options, basis, used_by}`. **kind `std`** = a value with a NAMED source in this repo or the rate pack (wall thickness and slab thickness from the pack descriptions `Blockwork Wall 150mm`, `RC Slab 250mm`; plate thickness 1/4 in from the truss section's own label); **kind `guess`** = typical practice, to be confirmed. Today 9 keys are consumed by a builder (`door.*`, `synth.*`, `fan.depth`, `net.thickness`, `panel.plane`, `section.spacing`); the others (window, stair, railing, ramp, pile, ceiling, curtain wall, column/beam/footing fallbacks, wall height) are ready for the builders that need them and are visible and settable now.
**Resolution (`modeller/dxf2defaults.js`).** The drawing set's `@DEFAULT=<dotted.key>,<value>` marking (BIMCompiler #219; metadata in the DXF, no sidecar, `@BY=user`) beats a choice made in the editor beats the registry value. A modules calls `DF.use(key)` ONLY when it applies a default (nothing drawn, nothing stated); the yellow rows come from the registry for exactly those keys: kind `default` (a guess or standard value in force), `chosen` (editor), `stated` (the drawing set); each row carries its basis (`std` | `guess`), element and unit. A stated value that is not a non-negative number is refused: the default is used and the problem listed (`defaults_invalid`). An unknown key throws (no silent default for a typo).
**Surfaces.** Editor: an "Element defaults" section (collapsed) lists ALL settings grouped by element with the value in force, the standard|guess tag and where it came from; highlighted = used in this model; Set writes `@DEFAULT`, the remove button returns to the default. CLI: `--default key=value` writes the marking into the first plan sheet; `verdict.json` carries `defaults_table` (all 40 with value, source, kind, used) and `defaults_invalid`. The page loads `element_defaults.json` with its scripts. Doors, synth, panels (fans, nets) and sections now read their defaults from the registry (their former literals are the registry's values).
**Witnesses.** W-DEFAULTS DF1-DF11 (the registry is complete and consistent, coverage of every class the bill knows plus stairs/railings/ramps/piles/ceilings/curtain walls, `std` reserved for named sources, resolution order, rows only for applied defaults, invalid and unknown refused, table, integration through the doors, falsifier: editing the JSON changes the door), W-DXF-EDEFAULTS E1-E5 (page: 40 rows grouped, door.height highlighted as a guess, Set 2.4 -> a 2.4 m door and `@DEFAULT=door.height,2.4` in the saved sheet, re-open keeps it, remove returns to 2.1, bad input refused), W-CLI C36 (the table in verdict.json; `--default` changes the real shed's 4 doors to 2.4 m with a stated row).

## L19 The shed has no Rosetta Stone: prior art, research, and the strategy (2026-10-18)
**Prior art in our own work (what the strategy builds on).**
- **Rosetta Stones** (`bim-compiler/docs/archive/TheRosettaStoneStrategy.md`): 35 real buildings decomposed into reference DBs; a compiled building is certified only if every element lands at the SAME COORDINATES as the reference. Four tiers: VOCABULARY (right parts), PLACEMENT (right places, 1 mm), INTEGRITY (the building works: sits on, inside, no clashes), COMPOSITIONAL (proven words in valid sentences, for buildings with no reference DB: provenance, fragment fidelity, spatial invariants, containment; the "Rosetta Dictionary"). Position is the metric, never dimensions first.
- **DXF2BIM full circle** (`DXF2BIM_TechnicalNote.md`, lane §L1-L5): BIM -> standard 2D set -> import -> IFC4, compared with the source model (Duplex: R1a precision, R1b recall, every check run once with its rule deliberately broken). Honest limit recorded there: one round-trip building and one partial independent drawing; "any DXF" is not claimed.
- **This session's shed** is the first REAL independent drawing at scale: eight sheets, 448 bodies, a bill, every unknown a yellow row. But there is no reference model for it, so it is Tier 4 (compositional) territory, not Tier 1-2.
**What the shed is (measured on the drawing).** 50 ft x 129.5 ft (15.24 m x 39.5 m) clear plan, 10 ft column pitch (bay 120 in), eave 11 ft above ground, ridge 18.7 ft, roof pitch about 17 degrees (the truss section says 17.5), 5 in / 10 in brick gable ends with 55 in fans, 8 ft brick side walls with wire-net above to 10 ft, EC pads and doors in the end bays, RCC columns 12 x 12 in on 4 x 5 ft footings, trusses of 1.5 in angles. The title block places it in Bangladesh.
**Research on how such sheds are designed (sources; confidence stated).**
- Bangladesh practice: "almost all broiler shed in our country are open sided natural house", cross-ventilation through WIRE NET, polythene sheet on the side walls against cold/storm/rain (Sher-e-Bangla Agricultural University lecture, https://www.sau.edu.bd/public/uploads/pdf/dept/class_lecture/24_2020-08-11_5f32f1c00ed0c.pdf). This supports the owner's answer that the ZIGZAG hatch is a net. A Bangladeshi consultant's slides give a typical open house of 15-20 ft width and a 3 ft wall on an east-west axis (https://www.slideshare.net/slideshow/broiler-management-by-dr-md-ramzan-ali/60958985; slide text, medium confidence).
- General tropical guidance (India; TNAU, https://agritech.tnau.ac.in/expert_system/poultry/Poultry%20House%20Construction.html): open-sided houses should not be wider than 22-25 ft (wider needs ridge ventilation); environmentally controlled houses 40 ft or wider with exhaust fans; eaves 6-7 ft, centre 10-12 ft; side wall 1-1.5 ft for the open-sided type; overhang at least 3.5 ft; gable roof usual; concrete floor, foundation 1-1.5 ft above ground; door preferred 6 x 2.5 ft opening outward; broilers about 1 sq ft per bird.
- Tunnel / evaporative-pad houses (Mississippi State Extension P2778, Aviagen tunnel-ventilation poster, Univ. of Georgia pad rule quoted by The Poultry Site, OMAFRA): air speed 2.5-3 m/s (492-591 ft/min) at bird height, modern houses 600-700 ft/min; pad area is sized by TUNNEL FAN CAPACITY (about 1 sq ft of 6 in pad per 350 cfm), not by house size; fans at one end, pads at the other; a 12 m x 122 m (40 x 400 ft) house is the Iowa State reference. Cobb example house 14 m wide, 150 m long, 2.88 m average height.
- NOT found: a Bangladesh shed standard (DLS / BLRI), truss spacing or roof pitch for poultry houses, taka construction rates. These stay yellow.
**Reading of the shed against the research.** At 50 ft wide, 11 ft eave and 18.7 ft ridge it is NOT the traditional Bangladeshi open-sided house (15-20 ft, 3 ft wall) but a wide, tall house with end fans, side nets and EC pads: the open-sided cross-ventilated practice scaled up with tunnel features. That tells us what to expect of other drawings of the type: gable-end fans, net bands above low brick walls, door pairs at the ends, pads in the end bays.
**Strategy (no reference library exists for 'Shed'; we do not invent one).**
1. **A typology, not a stone.** `shed` is a parametric TYPE with cited ranges (width, length, eave, ridge, pitch, bay, wall heights, fan diameters, pad placement), each carrying its source and a confidence, kept as data beside `element_defaults.json`. It is used for three things only: (a) proposing defaults when a drawing is silent (always yellow, basis = the citation), (b) PLAUSIBILITY advisories ("a 50 ft open-sided house is outside the 22-25 ft guidance: tunnel features expected"), (c) naming the set's expected components so a missing one is reported (a shed with fans but no pad drawn). It is never ground truth.
2. **Full circle on a real drawing.** Run our own loop backwards: the 448-body IFC4 -> the std2d writer (BIM -> 2D export, lane §L5) -> compare the regenerated plan/elevation positions with the ORIGINAL drawing (Tier 2 placement on the 2D side: 1 mm on every column, footing, wall run, hatch loop, fan centre). Where they agree the reading is closed-loop consistent; where they differ the product misread. This is the Rosetta test with the original drawing as the answer key, and it needs no reference model.
3. **Promote the shed to a Stone once the owner signs it off.** When the owner confirms or corrects the yellow rows, the resulting IFC becomes the first Stone of type Shed (position metric, six gates), and the next shed drawing is certified against a TYPE, not guessed.
4. **Compositional gates for what has no answer key.** Provenance (every body traces to a drawn entity, a stated setting or a citation), spatial invariants (roof covers the structure, columns stand on footings, fans inside openings, doors inside walls), containment. These are the G7 gates of the Rosetta Dictionary applied to a drawing-derived building.
5. **Next builders in this order of evidence on the shed:** door openings cut into wall panels (spatial invariant: a door inside a wall), the four leafless swings, the EC pad (placement rule from the pad-sizing literature once the owner gives the fan capacity), then windows/stairs/railings when a drawing carries them.

## L20 The Building Type setting (owner, 2026-10-18: "a Building Type value as part of the setting, i.e. 'Farming Shed' ... how we organise our reference component library")
**Setting.** `building.type` is a registry key (text; options `General`, `Farming Shed`; default `General`) stated like any other setting: `@DEFAULT=building.type,Farming Shed` in the drawing set, the Building type selector at the top of the editor's Element defaults section, or `--default "building.type=Farming Shed"`. It is always visible as a yellow row (a guess until stated); an unknown type falls back to `General` (no invented values).
**Type-specific defaults (`element_defaults.json` `building_types`).** Each type lists its SOURCES (label, url, confidence), its OVERRIDES (registry key -> value + a cited basis; `Farming Shed`: door 6 x 2.5 ft = 1.83 x 0.762 m and roof overhang 3.5 ft = 1.0668 m, TNAU poultry house construction), the components it EXPECTS (`IfcFan`, `IfcCovering` nets and pads, `IfcDoor`, `IfcRoof`, `IfcSlab`) and TYPICAL ranges for plausibility advisories (open-sided width 15-25 ft, environment-controlled 40 ft and wider, eave 6-7 ft, centre 10-12 ft, air speed 492-700 ft/min). Resolution order: the drawing's `@DEFAULT` > an editor choice > the building type's override > the base registry value; the table and the yellow rows say `type` and name the type in their basis. The typology never overrides what is drawn or stated, and is not ground truth.
**The reference component library is organised by Building Type.** The library index (`dxf2bim-library-index/1`: opening families, wall types, geometry map, published as versioned shards on OCI) gains a `building_types` list per family and per component, so a set whose `building.type` is `Farming Shed` is offered the components that belong to it (poultry-house door, axial extraction fan, wire net, evaporative pad, brick low wall) before generic ones, and a component that exists only for another type is not offered. A family tagged `General` is offered to every type. The tag is data in the index (BIMCompiler `export_library_subset.js`), so the library is regenerated and re-published only with the owner's go: this section is the spec; the setting and the type overrides are built and witnessed now.
**Witnesses.** W-DEFAULTS DF12-DF17 (every type has sources and cited overrides whose key and kind match the registry; General keeps the base guess; `@DEFAULT=building.type,Farming Shed` switches the cited values on and the rows name the type; a stated value still beats the type; an unknown type falls back to General; through the doors), W-CLI C37 on the real shed (`--default "building.type=Farming Shed"`: the 4 doors are 1.83 m, source `type`).

## L21 Rosetta full circle on the shed: 448-body IFC -> std2d -> compare with the ORIGINAL sheets at 1 mm (2026-10-18, spec before code; §L19 strategy item 2)
**Issue this proves or disproves.** Whether our reading of the eight real shed sheets is closed-loop consistent: the IFC4 the product built from them, written back to a standard 2D set by the BIM -> 2D writer (`std2d.js`, §L5), puts the things a plan shows at the SAME COORDINATES as the original drawing (Tier 2 placement, on the 2D side; the original drawing is the answer key, no reference model needed). Where they agree the reading is closed; where they differ the product misread, or the writer cannot draw it, and each difference is a listed row, never tuned away.
**Input (nothing hand-built).** The 448-body IFC is the one the product CLI writes for the exact `ALLARGS` line of `witness_dxf_cli.js` C31, extracted through the browser path (`ifc_extract_core.js`, sql.js), then `std2d.writeStdSet`. The original sheet is `0_FLOOR_PLAN.dxf` (inches; the regenerated set is millimetres in the same world frame, so the original coordinate x 25.4 is the comparable value; no translation is fitted).
**Known blocker found by reading the code (the shed is not a Duplex).** `std2d.loadModel` rule R2 takes the storey that holds `IfcFooting` as the FOUNDATION storey and the grid storey as the first walled storey ABOVE it. The shed is one storey ("Level 1", elevation 0) holding footings (z -1.45..-1.14 m), columns and walls, so no storey is above the foundation, `grid_storey` is null and `planStoreys` throws (`Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'elev')`). Proposed rule R2' (BIMCompiler, with the Python oracle, NOT patched here because `modeller/dxf2bim/std2d.js` is a verbatim copy): when the only walled storey is the footing storey, there is no foundation storey (`foundation = null`, `§STOREY_SINGLE footings share the walled storey`), the grid storey is that storey. Every model that has a lower foundation storey is unchanged (the new branch is reachable only where the old code threw). LANDED as BIMCompiler #220 (py + js, W-R2PRIME fails before / passes after; Duplex log unchanged, shed 381 lines py==js); the witness now plans the shed with no hook.
**What a plan can answer (the comparison set; the rest is an account, not a failure).** The writer cuts the plan 1.2 m above the storey, so it can answer: columns (32 `Col` loops), the building grid (the `C L` centre lines), the walls cut at 1.2 m, the doors. Footings (below the cut), beams, roof, slab, fans, truss members/plates and nets (above or beside it) are not plan content: they are listed per class with the reason, and the elevation/section/beam/foundation sheets are not compared here (next).
**Comparisons (each names what it proves).**
- F1 PREMISE: the unmodified writer cannot plan the shed (the throw above), so the hook is needed and R2' is a real finding.
- F2 COLUMNS: each of the 32 original `Col` loops matches exactly one regenerated `S-COLS` loop, one-to-one, all four bbox sides within 1 mm. Proves column placement closes the loop.
- F3 GRID: each of the 18 original `C L` lines matches exactly one regenerated `A-GRID` line on its perpendicular coordinate within 1 mm (14 vertical, 4 horizontal; line length is annotation, not position). Proves the writer's derived grid is the drawing's grid.
- F4 DOORS: the 4 regenerated `A-DOOR` outlines equal the 4 door bodies (built from the original `Rod` swing symbols) within 1 mm.
- F5 WALLS: each regenerated `A-WALL-FULL` contour lies inside a wall body's plan bbox (1 mm) and its two faces across the wall thickness equal the body's within 1 mm; contours may be shorter than the bbox along the run (a panel whose outline is not a rectangle at the cut height), reported as a row.
- F6 ACCOUNT: every body class either has regenerated plan content or a stated reason; wall bodies without a contour are listed by guid.
- F7 CONTROL (the witness can fail): one regenerated column moved 2 mm and one deleted must each turn F2 red; one grid line moved 2 mm must turn F3 red.
**Output.** `modeller/tests/logs/witness_dxf_fullcircle.log`, one PASS/FAIL line per check with the numbers (worst deviation, counts). Run: `NODE_PATH=<dir with web-ifc, sql.js> node modeller/tests/witness_dxf_fullcircle.js` (needs `~/Projects/SYSNOVA/DXF2BIM/ShedDrawing/sheets/`).

### L21.1 Results (2026-10-18; `modeller/tests/witness_dxf_fullcircle.js`, log `modeller/tests/logs/witness_dxf_fullcircle.log`, 8/8 PASS incl. F8 of §L21.2, read line by line)
- **F1** unmodified `writeStdSet` throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'elev')` on the shed: R2' is a real blocker, not a convenience. The witness applies R2' through `premodel` (`foundation 'Level 1' -> null; grid_storey = 'Level 1'`); the Modeller's Export button still cannot plan a single-storey model with footings until R2' lands in BIMCompiler (std2d.py + std2d.js + oracle). R2' is BIMCompiler #220 (synced here from its branch commit; re-run `sync.sh` on the master merge commit).
- **F2 columns** 32 original `Col` loops <-> 32 `S-COLS`, one-to-one, worst bbox deviation **0.0033 mm**.
- **F3 grid** 18 original `C L` lines (14 vertical, 4 horizontal) <-> 18 `A-GRID`, worst **0.0133 mm**: the writer's derived grid IS the drawing's grid (10 ft pitch, 3048 mm; the 4 horizontal lines at 5080 mm).
- **F4 doors** 4 <-> 4, worst **0.005 mm**. Not run: the 4 leafless 21.7 in swings are still listed, not built (and the writer logs `GAP:door_swing` x4 and `GAP:opening_no_host` x4: the IFC has no wall-host relation for the doors, which is the open item "door openings cut into the wall panels").
- **F5 walls** 35 contours, each inside a wall body's plan bbox, thickness faces worst **0.004 mm**. 5 contours are shorter along the run than the body bbox (max 207.8 mm): VERIFIED on the mesh, the two North panels `F9`/`F7` (and the South pair) have a CURVED vertical edge in the IFC (vertices from x -664219.7 at z 0.61 bulging to -664031.5 at z 2.29), so the 1.2 m cut is narrower than the bbox. The panel outline comes from the hatch loop of the elevation: a drawn fact, not a writer error.
- **F6 account** 11 classes explained (columns, walls, doors drawn; footings below the cut; beams, roof, slab, fans, truss members/plates, nets are not plan content). 4 of the 41 wall bodies (the gable-end panels, z 3.35 to 5.70) are above the 1.2 m cut and have no contour, as they must.
- **F7 control** a column moved 2 mm (worst 2.00), a column deleted, a grid line moved 2 mm (1.99): each turns its check red.
**Reading.** On the plan side the shed closes the loop at far better than 1 mm for columns, grid and doors, and walls agree across the thickness. Not yet compared (next): the four elevations, the roof plan and the truss section against their original sheets, and beams/footings, which the writer has no sheet for. Those need new writer content, not a tolerance change.

### L21.2 Doors and the opening cut (2026-10-18; RESUME next-step 2, measured, W-FULLCIRCLE F8)
**Finding: there is nothing to cut.** The drawn wall panels already leave the door gaps: none of the 4 door bodies overlaps any of the 41 wall bodies in 3D, and each door stands in a gap of its own wall line bounded by wall bodies on both sides (clearance left|right in mm: 1D7 62|13, 2D1 62|13, 33B 762|76, 353 76|762; the end doors are bounded by the gable-end panel on one side). The earlier "doors cutting their opening in the wall panel: not done (the panel runs through the gap)" (§L16, §L17) was a precaution, not a measurement; for this shed it is closed by F8. If a future drawing's panel does run through a door, F8's overlap test is the detector and the cut is built then (a void in the panel mesh, `IfcOpeningElement` + `IfcRelVoidsElement` + `IfcRelFillsElement`), not before.
**Host relation stays unstated, on purpose.** The writer logs `GAP:opening_no_host` x4 because the IFC carries no `IfcRelFillsElement` for the doors; the only wall candidates are the neighbours across a gap, and naming one would invent a host. Listed, not filled.
**The four 21.7 in swings stay listed, now with the evidence for not building them.** They are unlabelled (only 3 `Door` texts exist), sweep 75.3 degrees (the three labelled doors sweep 87.7 / 90.9, a quarter turn), and their hinges sit 200-260 mm off the wall line (e.g. hinge y -95,502 mm against the wall line -95,698 mm). They read as hatches/louvres, not doors, so a default door built on them would be a guess about WHAT the object is, not a yellow value for a known object. They remain `GAP:door_closed_unknown`, now reclassified as "possible non-door swing"; the owner can state them with `--layer`/`@SIZE` if they are doors.

## L22 Typology plausibility advisories (2026-10-18; RESUME next-step 3; spec before code; §L19 strategy 1(b)+(c), §L20 `typical` + `expect`)
**Issue this proves or disproves.** Whether the product can say, from cited type data alone, "this drawing reads as a <type> of such a class, and these expected parts are (not) in it", without ever overriding what is drawn or stated. The advisories are information rows, never blocking, never a default (defaults are §L18/§L20); they exist so a person (or the next drawing's reader) sees a measurement next to the cited range.
**Module.** `modeller/dxf2typology.js` (pure, Node + browser): `advise(els, typeName, ref)` -> `{ type, measured, rows }`. `typeName` is the type in force (the same resolution as `dxf2defaults`); a type with no `typical`/`expect` (`General`, unknown) returns no rows. `els` = the built bodies (`class`, `kind`, `vertices` in metres, `z0`, `z1`).
**Measurements (every one from bodies, units stated).**
- width / length: the shorter / longer plan extent of the bbox of all `IfcColumn` bodies (outer faces); no columns -> not measured, one row says so.
- eave: highest `z1` of any `IfcBeam`, else of any `IfcColumn`, minus the storey datum 0; ridge ("centre"): highest `z1` of any `IfcRoof`, minus 0; none -> not measured. (The shed, measured: columns' outer faces 15.54 m x 39.93 m = 51 x 131 ft [§L19's 50 x 129.5 ft are the grid-line distances; the column faces add one column width]; eave = the highest beam top 3.4036 m = 11.2 ft [17 beams carry the 12 in label depth, §L10.7]; ridge 5.704 m = 18.7 ft.)
**Rows (code, level always `advisory`, each with measured value, cited range, source label).**
- `TYP:width_class`: width in ft against `width_open_sided_ft` [lo, hi]: inside -> "open-sided range"; above hi and below `width_environment_controlled_ft_min` -> "between the open-sided and the environment-controlled class"; at or above the minimum -> "environment-controlled class: tunnel features (end fans, evaporative pads) expected"; below lo -> "narrower than the open-sided range".
- `TYP:eave_range`, `TYP:centre_range`: measured ft against `eave_ft`, `centre_ft`: "inside" or "above/below the cited range by N ft".
- `TYP:expected_missing` (one row per class): each class in `expect` with zero bodies. `TYP:expected_present` is one summary row with the counts.
- `TYP:tunnel_no_pad`: width in the environment-controlled class AND at least one `IfcFan` body AND no body of kind `pad`: "fans drawn, no evaporative pad drawn" (the EC pads are the §L17 owner item: nothing is invented, the row only reports).
**Surface.** CLI: `verdict.json` gains `typology` (`{type, measured, rows}`) and the log prints `§TYPOLOGY` lines; with type `General` it prints `§TYPOLOGY type=General rows=0`. Page/editor display is the page-flow step (next-step 6).
**Witnesses (`witness_dxf_typology.js`, each names its issue).** TY1 General and an unknown type give no rows (the typology never speaks without a stated type); TY2 a 20 ft synthetic house is in the open-sided range, no tunnel row; TY3 a 50 ft house is the environment-controlled class; TY4 a house between the ranges (30 ft) gets the "between" wording; TY5 an expected class with no body is reported by name, and a present one is not; TY6 `tunnel_no_pad` fires with fans and no pad kind, not without fans, not with a pad; TY7 eave/centre inside vs above the range with the exact excess; TY8 units: the registry's feet are converted at 0.3048 and the row shows both; TY9 determinism (twice the same); TY10 falsifier: edit the registry's `width_open_sided_ft` and the row changes (the data, not code, decides); CLI C-run on the REAL shed with `--default "building.type=Farming Shed"`: width 51.0 ft / length 131.0 ft, environment-controlled class, eave 11.2 ft (4.2 above 6-7), centre 18.7 ft (6.7 above 10-12), expected parts all present, `tunnel_no_pad` fires; and without `--default` there are 0 rows.

### L21.3 Elevations, roof, section (2026-10-18; W-FULLCIRCLE F9)
Heights are frame-free (the elevation sheets sit at their own x offsets; z follows the stated datum), so the elevation comparison is on HEIGHTS: every distinct height the four regenerated elevations draw on `A-WALL`/`A-ROOF` (7) must be a height some ORIGINAL elevation draws, at 1 mm. Result: 6 of 7 equal an original height (609.6, 1524.0, 3048.0, 3352.8, 4894.6, 5704.1; worst 0.04 mm); the 7th, 3328.5, is the synthetic roof sheet's underside (derived, §L13.2), 24.3 mm from the nearest drawn height, listed not forced. Observation for the owner, not decided in code: the South elevation's top line sits at 5791.7 mm while the West/East elevations and the section top out at 5704 / 5757 mm; the product built the roof from the section's 5704. Not compared, with reasons: the roof plan (`A-ROOF` is the synthetic roof, derived by construction), the regenerated `SECTION` (the writer cuts the BUILDING, the original `8_SECTION_AA_TRUSS` is a truss detail: different content), beams and footings (the writer has no sheet for them).

## L23 Reference library organised by Building Type: the index field (2026-10-18; RESUME next-step 4; spec §L20 "reference component library", built here)
**Data, not code.** `BIMCompiler scripts/library_building_types.json` states, per library record, which building types it belongs to: `{ "default": ["General"], "ad_opening_family": { "<family_id>": ["Farming Shed", ...] }, "ad_wall_type": { "<wall_type_id>": [...] } }`. A record not listed carries `default`. NOTHING is invented: today the library holds only the Duplex-class families (all `General`); the Farming Shed components named in §L20 (poultry-house door, axial extraction fan, wire net, evaporative pad, brick low wall) do not exist as library records, so none is tagged until they are published, and the mapping file starts with `default` only.
**Index.** `export_library_subset.js --full` adds `building_types` (sorted list of the types present, always including `General`) to the index and to every `ad_opening_family` / `ad_wall_type` row (`scripts/library_building_types.js` `tag`, pure). A tag naming a record that does not exist is REFUSED (a typo would silently never match). Same inputs -> byte-identical output.
**Offer rule (`modeller/dxf2library_types.js` `offered(rows, type)`, pure).** For the type in force: records tagged with that type first (in index order), then records tagged `General`; a record tagged only for ANOTHER type is not offered; no type or an unknown type = `General` only. The republish to OCI is the owner's go (not done).
**Witnesses.** W-LIBTYPES LT1 default tagging, LT2 explicit tag, LT3 a tag naming a missing record is refused, LT4 determinism, LT5 `offered` order and exclusion (type first, General after, other-type excluded, unknown type = General only), LT6 falsifier: removing a tag changes what is offered; LT7 the real library index (`--full` into a temp dir) carries `building_types` on every family and wall row, and `counts` equal the untagged export's.

## L24 One solid helper, one body-kinds table, builders gated on evidence, the Typology section (2026-10-18; RESUME next-steps 5 and 6)
**Done (output-identical).** `dxf2prep.js` now exports `Solid` (`BOX`, `vol`, `outward`, `box`): the box topology, signed volume and outward winding that `dxf2doors.js`, `dxf2section.js` and `dxf2synth.js` each copied. Proof the refactor changed nothing: the whole-shed run (C31 line) before and after gives byte-identical `bodies.json` and `bill.json`, and an `model.ifc` that differs only in the FILE_NAME timestamp; W-SOLID SO1-SO4 (volume +24, mirrored corners still outward, flipped winding seen as negative and repaired, no shared mutable face array) and every geometry witness (doors, defaults, section, synth, panels, bill, hatch, pair, pure, account, bodies, beams, cli) are green. `dxf2prep.js` also holds `BODY_KINDS`, the ONE list of layer-typeable body kinds; the structure judge and the editor read it, and W-KINDS K1 compares it with the marks grammar (`dxf2marks.js` LAYER_TYPES.BODY, a verbatim BIMCompiler copy that cannot import it) so drift between the two repos fails a test. A new kind now touches: `KIND` + `BODY_KINDS` (dxf2prep), its builder, the marks grammar (BIMCompiler), the bill/pack class mapping.
**Builders for windows, stairs, railings, ramps, piles, ceilings, curtain walls: NOT built, by evidence.** §L19 orders them "by evidence on the drawings" and the shed carries none: the plan's `Win` layer holds 26 five-inch arcs and 18 + 8 lines of 55 / 56 in with no window text anywhere (a layer NAME is not evidence of what is drawn), there are no stair, rail, ramp, pile, ceiling or curtain-wall symbols. A builder written without a drawing to read would be a guess about the drawing convention, and could only be witnessed on a fixture I invented. They wait for step 7 (a real BricsCAD set that has them); the defaults for all of them are already in the registry (§L18), so the builders land as data-driven code on that evidence.
**Step 6 (page).** The editor gains a collapsed "Typology" section (after Element defaults): the §L22 advisories for the building type in force, with the cited source as the tooltip; it appears only when a type with data is stated. W-DXF-EDEFAULTS E7 (Farming Shed lists advisories incl. missing expected classes) and E8 (General: no section at all); the other browser witnesses (struct, complete, panels_page, sizes, attention, ticks) are green; `sw.js` v104 + the script lists carry `dxf2typology.js`. Not done: showing the library records a type is offered (needs the republished index, owner's go) and the film re-cut (a media job on the finished page flow).

## L25 What the shed's own sheets point to (2026-10-18; owner: "if the shed does not have those, tackle what it points to; otherwise take from Duplex"; the shed is a 2D DWG -> DXF set with NO 3D reference, Duplex is the set with a 3D answer key)
**Scan of all 11 sheets (not only the 8 the pipeline reads).** Windows, stairs, railings, ramps, piles, curtain walls: none drawn. Windows/doors in general are already solved on the Duplex side (wall pipeline: `elevdoor`/`glazing` clusters, `@OPENING`, sills and heads from elevations, library fills; Duplex full circle within 1 mm, §L5.7 / TechnicalNote), so no second window builder is written in the bodies path. What the shed does carry, in order of use:
1. **The `Win` layer is door frames, not windows.** On the elevations it holds rectangles 56 x 94 in and 32 x 94 in (the 2 x 27 in double leaf and a 33 in door); on the plan it holds the 55 in fan footprints. The existing ELEV layer type `elevdoor` is exactly this evidence. BUILT: `dxf2doors.js` clusters the `elevdoor` line work of each elevation, maps it through the aligned grids to a plan interval and z interval, and gives each door leaf (overlap >= 80 % of the leaf, the face nearest the door by the drawn columns' envelope) the DRAWN height and base; conflicting frames -> `GAP:door_height_conflict`; no frame -> the yellow `door.height` default as before. Real shed with `--layer elev:Win=elevdoor`: the two 33 in end doors 2.4384 m (96 in), the double leaf 2.3876 m (94 in), source `drawn`, `height_source: elevation`, the default row gone (W-CLI C38, control C38b without the layer = 2.1 m default).
2. **`6_SECTION_AA.dxf` (not read yet):** the cross-section: profile sheet roofing, FALSE CEILING, slats, polythene, compacted sand, 5 in brick walls, beams. New elements the product does not build: ceiling (IfcCovering/CEILING), floor build-up (sand, polythene). Next.
3. **`5_DETAILS.dxf` (not read yet):** footing schedule F-1 4'-0" x 5'-0" x 12" T, 12 mm @ 6" c/c; column schedule 12" x 12" above grade / 14" x 14" below, 6-16 mm main bars, 10 mm stirrups @ 5"/10"; grade beam 12" x 12"; concrete f'c = 24 MPa, fy = 500 MPa, casting ratios 1:1.5:3 (footing, column, GB) and 1:2:4 (floor). Material data for the existing bodies (the footing 12 in thickness agrees with the `--bodyz` we stated: 0.3048 m); the below-grade 14 in column section is a body not built. Next.
4. **EC Pad** is labelled on the plan and all elevations and detailed in `5_DETAILS` ("EC Pad Area", "Length may vary"): the owner item stays a yellow row until a length is stated.
5. The elevations disagree on the roof top (South 5792 mm against 5704 / 5757 mm elsewhere), kept as an observation (§L21.3).

## L26 FOCUS (owner, 2026-10-18): the whole shed in 3D on the canvas, metadata on every mesh, material colours from standard defaults, saved to a DB, opened in the Viewer on the 4D/5D page, and a film of it with chapter cards (the POC of how a common BricsCAD 2D set is handled). Everything else (false ceiling, pads, details sheet, more builders) is OFF the path until this runs.
**Measured start (2026-10-18, the C31 shed through IFC4 -> the usual extractor).** 448 elements, typed (class, drawing type name, PredefinedType, storey, discipline), 4 `element_type` rows. GAPS: (a) `elements_meta.material_name` is NULL for all 448 (the extractor inserts `null`; the IFC does carry 11 `IFCMATERIAL`s) ; (b) `material_rgba` is the SAME beige `0.920,0.900,0.850` for all 448 (web-ifc's white fallback), so the Viewer shows one colour; (c) no source/provenance metadata per mesh reaches the DB (drawn/derived/assumed, source sheet and handle, size sources); (d) the full 448-body shed has never been run through the PAGE (canvas) and the Viewer in one chain (the old film and W-DXF-COMPLETE use 3-24 bodies).
**Design (nothing invented; colours are a display convention, stated as such).**
1. **Material colours = a standard palette, data not code.** `modeller/synthetic_ref.json` gains `material_colour` (material name -> `[r,g,b]`): Reinforced concrete grey, Brick masonry brick red, Profiled metal roofing light steel blue, Netting green, Steel (members, plates, fan housing) dark steel, door leaf timber. Source of the part -> material mapping is the existing `materialOf` (§L13.3); a material with no palette entry gets a neutral grey and is LISTED (`no_colour`), never a silent random colour.
2. **IFC carries the colour the way every IFC viewer reads it:** one `IfcSurfaceStyle` (+ `IfcSurfaceStyleRendering`) per material, applied by an `IfcStyledItem` on each product's tessellated item (both writers: `dxf2ifc.js` headless, `bonsai_ifc.js` page). The unchanged extractor then reads the colour into `material_rgba` by its existing geometry-colour path.
3. **Material NAME and source metadata reach the DB (BIMCompiler extractor, additive):** `elements_meta.material_name` = the associated `IfcMaterial` name; the writers add `Pset_Dxf2Bim` (Provenance drawn|derived|assumed, SourceSheet, SourceHandle, Basis of each size) and the extractor reads psets named `Pset_Dxf2Bim` into a new table `element_dxf2bim(guid, name, value)`. Existing DBs are unchanged (the table is empty when no such pset exists).
4. **Canvas colour.** Material palette colour by default; the evidence tint (derived amber, assumed red, §L7.3) becomes an "Evidence colours" toggle, default OFF, so the person sees the shed as materials and can switch to see how much rests on assumptions. (Reversible; the previous default was the tint.)
5. **Save and open.** Export IFC4 -> the usual extractor -> `<name>_extracted.db` -> Viewer `?db=` : 3D with the palette, Find/info shows class, drawing type name, material, storey, provenance; the 4D (schedule) and 5D (cost) pages open on it; the Viewer's cost for the shed is the bill's (quantities from geometry x pack rates) or the Viewer's own estimate, whichever the Viewer computes, stated, not forced to agree.
**Witnesses (each names its issue).** W-SHED-CHAIN, in order, one browser run on the REAL eight sheets (SKIP without them): S1 the page imports the set and Convert puts 448 inserts on the canvas with `provenance.ifc` (class, type name, material) ; S2 canvas colours are the palette, distinct per material, and the toggle switches to amber/red; S3 Export IFC4 -> extractor: 448 elements, `material_name` set for all 448, `material_rgba` has exactly the palette's distinct colours, `element_dxf2bim` has a Provenance row for all 448; S4 the Viewer opens that DB, renders 448 meshes with those colours (pixel sample of a column vs a brick panel differs), Find "IfcWall" returns 41; S5 the 4D page and the 5D page open on it with non-empty content (task/cost counts logged, no console error); controls: removing a palette entry lists `no_colour`; a DB extracted from the old IFC (no styles) fails S3 (the witness can see the gap).
**Film (after the chain is green).** Chapter-card format of `film_title_cards.py` (CHAPTER n kicker coral, title off-white, line 2 coral, one plain line, dimmed backdrop, chapter tag + series tag), the real 2D sheets visible (client TEXT filtered), narration from run facts, assembled with `film_dxf2bim_bricscad_assemble.sh`. Chapters: 1 OPEN (the common BricsCAD sheets), 2 MEANING (suggestions, ticks, datum), 3 BODIES (the whole shed: columns, footings, beams, panels, fans, nets, doors, trusses, slab, roof), 4 THE MODEL (material colours + metadata + saved DB), 5 THE VIEWER (3D, 4D, 5D, cost).

Every spoken number is a `§FILM_FACT` of the recording run. ### L26.3 NOTE FOR THE NEXT FILM TAKE / UPDATE (owner, 2026-10-18: "Good work! ... next time when doing or updating the film, move the rendered building to see around the building. Shadow ground mode, just Fly tour a bit, with Night mode and Alt-G ON")
In the Viewer chapter (chapter 5, v01/v02) the shed must not sit still: show it from around, in the Viewer's **Shadow ground** mode, with a short **Fly tour**, in **Night mode**, with **Alt+G ON** (the Viewer's global-illumination / ambient-occlusion pass, `viewer/effects_gi_poc.js`, off by default, built on its first press). Also move the Modeller canvas shot of chapter 3 (orbit once around the converted shed). Constraints from the v3 take: the Viewer loads wireframe first, then solid, so the beat starts only after the status line says DONE; Alt+G's first press builds the composer (allow it a few seconds before the beat); headless SwiftShader renders these slowly, so BEAT_MIN for the tour beats must be measured from the take, not assumed. Update the narration only with what is on screen (no claim about lighting beyond what the frame shows), re-run the leak check, and re-fit with `film_dxf2bim_shed_assemble.sh` (needs `FILM_CARDS_PY` with `FILM_SET=shed`).

### L26.4 Film v4 spec (2026-10-19; implements §L26.3; pending item 1 of the RESUME block)
**Issue it answers:** v3 shows the shed standing still in the Viewer (one frame angle, day, no ground). The take must show the building from around, in the Viewer's real modes, and prove each mode is ON from the page's own state, not from the narration.
**Beats (all driven by the Viewer's own keys, real key presses; no private hooks):**
- Chapter 3, after Convert + Fit: one full orbit of the Modeller camera about the shed (`A.controls.target`, Z up, 36 steps, 360 deg). Facts: camera azimuth before/after, steps.
- Chapter 5, after the Viewer status says DONE: `h` (Shadow + ground, first state = earth) -> `n` (Night) -> `Alt+G` (GI/AO pass; first press builds the composer: wait for `A._giComposerActive`, max 20 s) -> one orbit of the Viewer camera -> `l` (Fly tour, a few seconds; `A.flyActive` must be true) -> `l` again to stop -> Find walls as in v3 (41). The beats are `v01` (modes), `v01o` (orbit), `v01f` (fly), then `v02` unchanged.
- Facts logged per mode: `§FILM_FACT modes {"shadowGround":..,"night":..,"gi":..}`, `fly {"active":..,"actions":..}`, camera position change (> 1 m) for the orbit. A mode that is not ON at its check makes the recorder exit non-zero (no film from a take where the mode silently failed).
**Narration:** only what the frame shows: shadow and ground, night, the tour. No claim about lighting quality. `BEAT_MIN` for the new beats is measured from the take (SwiftShader is slow), not assumed. Leak check (client text) and the assembler's witness lines are read as for v3. The Malay track follows after v4 EN is accepted.
**Results (2026-10-19, take rec8; `~/Videos/DXF2BIM_Chapter_BricsCAD_Shed_to_Viewer_1920x1080_25fps_2026-10-19_v4.mp4`, 4 min 33 s, 6840 frames = master, -17.9 LUFS, no silence >= 2 s).** Beats as built: `v01` Shadow ground (`h`, earth) -> `v01o` render-paced orbit of the Viewer camera (36 steps, 38 s on SwiftShader) -> `v01n` Night (`n`) -> `v01f` Fly tour (`l`, 4x via `cycleWalkSpeed`, tour at 48 %) -> `v01g` Alt+G (`modes {"shadowGround":"earth","night":true,"gi":true}`) -> Find 41 as before. Findings that shaped it: (a) with GI on, SwiftShader frames are so slow and the tour's dt is clamped to 0.1 s per frame that the tour stood at 4 % after 30 s; so the tour flies in Night + shadow and Alt+G is switched on after it (all three modes are ON at the end, checked from page state; the recorder throws if one is not); (b) the orbit steps wait for two painted frames, else the screencast shows a still. Modeller canvas orbit (36 steps) is only about 4 s: short but present. Not done: Malay track, `Pset_Dxf2Bim` cut of the info panel.

## L28 The Viewer info panel shows `Pset_Dxf2Bim` (2026-10-19; RESUME item 3)
**Issue:** the per-mesh provenance (drawn / derived / assumed, source sheet and handle, size basis) reaches the DB table `element_dxf2bim` (§L26.1) but the person looking at an element in the Viewer cannot see it. **Design:** a block `#info-dxf2bim` in `#info-panel` ("From the drawing set"), filled by `viewer/info_dxf2bim.js` on the plain canvas pick (`picking.js`) and on the Find pick (`find_erp_push.js` `_show4DWindow` seam): one renderer, two call sites, the `info_4d_panel.js` pattern. It reads `element_dxf2bim` for the picked guid, shows the known keys in a fixed order, then any other keys sorted; a DB without the table or without rows for that guid keeps the block HIDDEN (never an empty box). Nothing is computed or defaulted here; every line is a stored row. `sw.js` precaches the module, CACHE_VERSION v1638.
**Witness W-INFO-DXF2BIM (`viewer/tests/witness_info_dxf2bim.js`, SKIP without the local shed DB):** I1 the shed DB (448 elements): for 3 picked guids of different classes the block is visible and shows `Provenance` and `SourceSheet` equal to the table rows; I2 every one of the 448 guids renders (renderer returns true) and the shown Provenance equals the row; I3 control, the Duplex DB (no `element_dxf2bim` rows): the block stays hidden for 3 picks; I4 in the real Viewer page on the shed DB, the Find pick of an IfcWall makes `#info-dxf2bim` visible with a Provenance line (page, not only the module).
